# Roadmap Archive (full history)

> Verbatim snapshot of `ROADMAP.md` taken 2026-08-04, before it was compacted into an index. Nothing here is lost; look up a row by its number. Do NOT read this file wholesale -- it is 200KB and reading it is what stalled the loop.

# Enhancement Roadmap

A maturity/enhancement backlog layered on the shipped **v0.1.1**, grounded in
[`SPEC.md`](SPEC.md). The product is already complete and tested (134 tests
green through iter-02); everything here is a small, self-contained increment
that raises the bar of an existing layer — L2 scout perception, L1 loop
execution, L0 resilience/observability, or CLI/DX — without breaking the public
contracts. Each iteration of the dev loop selects exactly one row.

Ordering is by leverage on the product's thesis ("a proactivity layer that is
resilient by design"), balanced against blast radius. Seeded from both PM scouts
(new-capability lens + hardening/DX lens) across iters 01–03 and a fresh read of
`SPEC.md`. Net-new rows (#17+) surfaced mid-loop by a scout may jump the queued
backlog when they close a docs-vs-code honesty gap on a public repo (the standing
iter-01 tie-break rule: an integrity fix outranks a net-new capability). Applied
iters 01, 03, and 05; iters 04, 06, and 07 went net-new (`pla runs`, `pla explain`,
then `pla trace`) because no live 30s-falsifiable docs-vs-code contradiction existed
those iterations. iter-08 returns to the queued backlog, applying #6 (retry telemetry)
to complete the observability arc runs → explain → trace → retry-visibility. iter-09
shipped #5 (DependencyCollector), iter-10 shipped #21 (net-new `--workspace` guard), and
iter-11 returns to the queued backlog with #8 (WorkingTreeCollector) — its consecutive-collector
objection now spent by iter-10's CLI verb. The integrity tie-break is now a depleted resource: both iter-07
scouts independently re-verified the verb lists and version sources are all consistent,
so a net-new capability need not defer to an integrity fix. iter-12 returns to the queued backlog with #11 (`pla scan --format {table,json,markdown}`) — picked over the strong net-new alternative of range-guarding `auto_dispatch_min_score` (recorded as #22): iter-10 already shipped that exact 'self-inconsistency guard' shape, iter-09/11 were L2 collectors, so a new CLI output format diversifies off both recent grounds AND gives the isolated Tester three end-to-end stdout branches (see iter-12 pm.md). iter-13 opens the one architectural layer untouched since the initial commit — the L1 ACT sandbox — shipping #9 (read-only `search_files`), picked over the strong hardening alternative #22 (range-guard `auto_dispatch_min_score`): #22's queue-edge deferral is spent, but iter-10 already shipped that exact self-inconsistency-guard shape, so a 2nd guard in 4 iters repeats it, whereas #9 is the only genuinely-new capability in either slate and diversifies off the L2→CLI→L2→CLI tail (see iter-13 pm.md). iter-14 finally ships #22 (range-guard `auto_dispatch_min_score` with `Field(ge=0.0)`), picked over the strong new-capability alternative of a `pla signals` perception inspector: #22 closes an actual autonomy-bypass (a negative threshold auto-dispatches the whole non-sensitive slate) on a safety-first product, it lands on the config-validation layer untouched since iter-03 (whereas `signals` would be a 3rd CLI touch after iters 10/12), and iter-13's remaining objection — 'iter-10 already shipped that guard shape' — is now three iterations stale and factually distinct (iter-10 = a filesystem-existence check on a CLI arg; #22 = a pydantic Field bound in the config layer). Lowest-blast-radius clean commit available: a one-line `Field` bound, default 4.0 unchanged, demo byte-stable (see iter-14 pm.md). iter-15 ships the net-new `pla signals` perception inspector (recorded as #23), picked over the strongest alternatives -- Scout B's #4 CI+badge and Scout A's `TestPostureCollector`. CI is rejected THIS iteration on the public-repo prime directive: the factory cannot confirm the remote Action goes green before the push and a red badge is worse than none (latent CI-only failures: git user.email for the temp-repo test, uv.lock cross-platform resolution, 3.12-vs-3.13 drift) -- #4 stays Planned, deferred knowingly. A new collector carries real blast radius (perturbs the collector-registry count tests + fixture signal composition), whereas `pla signals` is fully offline-verifiable, additive, zero-blast-radius (cli.py + docs only), reuses `_collect` + the iter-10 `--workspace` guard verbatim, and has the richest deterministic black-box surface -- the same profile that made runs/explain/trace clean wins. It completes the transparency arc signals -> scan -> explain -> trace; CLI last touched iter-12 so iter-14's '3rd CLI touch' objection is spent (see iter-15 pm.md). iter-16 ships #24 (`TestPostureCollector`, a new L2 collector emitting `kind="test_posture"` that flags project dirs with source files but zero tests), picked over Scout B's strongest alternative #13 (`make cov` DX target). A1 is new *perception* -- the product's headline thesis -- graded High value, vs #13's Low-Med DX nicety whose dead `pytest-cov` dep is not a docs-vs-code contradiction (no README/SPEC claims coverage runs), so the integrity tie-break does not fire. L2 collectors are 4 iters cold (coldest *productive perception* layer; the consecutive-collector objection is spent since iters 12-15 were CLI/L1/config/CLI). Crucially the iter-14/15 deferral reason -- 'a 7th collector perturbs the collector-registry count tests + fixture composition' -- is FALSIFIED this iteration by reading `tests/test_collectors.py`: `test_returns_all_four_types` is a membership assertion (`assert RecentFilesCollector in types`) with NO total-count check, its four sibling registry tests only iterate `all_collectors()`, and iters 09/11 already added the 5th/6th collectors with zero registry breakage; the fixture concern is a Tester-authoring note (assert on synthetic tmp workspaces, never the in-repo fixture) and demo stays byte-stable (scripted synthesizer matches on tag, not prompt content). A wrong deferral objection is retired, not perpetuated (see iter-16 pm.md). iter-17 ships #10 (`append_file` L1 tool, `79dd40f`) on the coldest productive-execution layer and lowest blast radius, deferring Scout A's `pla watch` as the explicit 'top backlog row.' iter-18 returns to that backlog row and ships #25 (net-new `pla watch` verb wiring the shipped-but-unreachable `scheduler.run_periodic` into the CLI), picked over Scout B's strongest alternative B1 (enforce 'collectors never raise -> []' at the `_collect` orchestration boundary). B1 is genuine defense-in-depth, but every current built-in collector already degrades to `[]` (SPEC 4.1), so it guards a hypothetical future/third-party raising collector -- latent value -- whereas `pla watch` makes the product's NAMESAKE capability reachable (a proactive scan-on-a-schedule loop is why the product exists, yet `scheduler.py` -- the ONLY module untouched since the initial commit, its docstring literally 'the clock that makes proactivity proactive' -- had zero CLI entry point and `pla` could only fire once) AND revives dead-but-tested code. A1's two iter-17 objections are now BOTH spent: CLI cooled to 2 iters cold (last touch iter-15 `signals`) and L1 already received `append_file`, so the lower-blast-radius tie-break that beat watch no longer applies. It is offline-verifiable via bounded `--max-scans`/`--interval 0` (the injectable-sleep seam is already proven in `test_scheduler.py`), additive (no version bump), and stays OUT of `make demo`/CI (an unbounded watch would hang the demo). Scout A's A2 (`local`/ollama provider) touches the coldest module but needs a real network call at runtime + a monkeypatch-heavy Tester surface; A3 (`ReadmeGapCollector`) sits on the freshest layer (L2, iter-16) and near-copies `TestPostureCollector`; B3 (degradation-coverage) is test-only (starves the Engineer stage) and is B1's natural sibling. B1 + B3 + Scout B's deferred logging seam remain the strongest hardening backlog for a future iteration (see iter-18 pm.md). iter-19 finally ships that B1 (recorded as #26): isolate a raising collector at the `_collect` orchestration boundary so one misbehaving collector degrades to `[]` (logged at WARNING) instead of aborting all four front-door verbs. Two facts iter-18 under-weighted are now VERIFIED by reading source: (a) `_collect`'s docstring ASSERTS collectors 'yield fewer signals rather than aborting the scan' yet the bare loop does NOT enforce it -- a real docs-vs-code integrity gap (the standing tie-break: integrity outranks net-new); (b) `TestGracefulDegradation` hard-codes only 4 of the 7 collectors, so `dependencies`/`working_tree`/`test_posture` have ZERO never-raise coverage -- the 'every built-in already degrades to `[]`' premise that deferred B1 is unproven for 3/7, so the guard backstops REAL untested collectors, not just a hypothetical future one. B1 is the only hardening candidate that ships a PRODUCTION ARTIFACT (test-only hardening B2/B3 keep starving the Engineer stage -- #12 parked iters 14-18, #19 parked iters 04/07/11). Picked over Scout A's RECOMMENDED A1 `pla config` -- a 2nd consecutive CLI verb after iter-18 `watch` (Scout A's own only objection) -- kept as the top net-new backlog verb; over A2 `delete_file` (first destructive sandbox op, undemoed) and A3 `GitStateCollector` (novel but low-frequency AND itself a raise-prone 8th collector -- B1 is the enabling foundation for exactly that class of collector, so ship the guard first). B1's one real objection -- layer diversity, since cli.py was touched iters 15/18 -- is weak: it touches the `_collect` HELPER (the perception-collection orchestration seam, L2-adjacent), not the argparse parser or a new verb, and A1 would be a cli.py touch too, so CLI is contested regardless; value + a verified integrity gap outrank the diversity tie-break as in iters 07/14/16. Tiny (try/except + a one-line module logger + one SPEC sentence), additive, no version bump, byte-stable demo (see iter-19 pm.md). Scout B's B2 (make `TestGracefulDegradation` registry-driven + hostile-input cases) and B3 (adversarial sandbox symlink-parity suite) remain the strongest hardening backlog next -- B2 is now B1's natural proof-side companion. iter-20 ships #27 (net-new `GitStateCollector`, `kind="git_state"`) -- a new L2 collector that surfaces INTERRUPTED/dangling git operations (unfinished merge/rebase/cherry-pick/revert + detached HEAD) by reading `.git` marker files with `pathlib` (NO subprocess, NO network), the one class of git state blind to both existing git collectors (`git_activity` = committed past, `working_tree` = present diff/unpushed). Picked over Scout B's RECOMMENDED B1 (`py.typed` PEP 561 marker, #14): B1 is the safest possible commit but Low raw value (CLI-first tool, hypothetical import consumers) and NOT a docs-vs-code contradiction (SPEC S5 'type hints everywhere' is a code-quality invariant the code already satisfies; it never claims PEP 561 export -- the same reasoning the iter-16 lead used to keep the integrity tie-break DORMANT for the dead-dep angle), so it does not fire hard enough to jump a High-value new-perception pick; B1 stays parked as the ideal low-inventory filler. A1 is new *perception* (the product's headline thesis), diversifies onto the 3-iters-cold *productive perception* layer (off the CLI-heavy iters 15/18/19 tail), and -- decisively -- its ONE blocker just cleared: iter-19 deferred `GitStateCollector` BY NAME with the sequencing rule 'ship the containment before the risky thing it contains,' then shipped exactly that containment (the `_collect` per-collector isolation guard, #26, 398bf7f) -- so it is now the enabled follow-through, not a risk to sequence around (the textbook SEQUENCING-veto-goes-live-when-the-prerequisite-lands discipline). Distinct from every shipped collector by MECHANISM (pathlib marker reads, not a `git` subprocess) AND SIGNAL CLASS (interrupted operations, not commits/diffs), so it dodges the near-copy veto that killed `ReadmeGapCollector`. Picked over A2 `pla diff` / A3 `pla config` (both a 4th cli.py touch in six iters -- the layer-diversity headwind). Additive, no version bump; byte-stable demo (fixture workspace has no `.git`, so zero `git_state` signals). Scout B's B1 (`py.typed`), B2 (`make cov`) and B3 (registry-driven `TestGracefulDegradation`) remain the strongest hardening backlog next (see iter-20 pm.md). iter-21 ships a net-new read-only `find_files(pattern, path)` L1 ACT-sandbox tool (recorded as #28) -- recursive **basename glob discovery** so a dispatched loop can locate a file BY NAME ("where is the Makefile?", "find every test_*.py", "is there a pyproject.toml anywhere?"), the third member of the discovery triad (list-one-dir / grep-content / **find-by-name**) that `list_files`/`search_files`/`read_file` cannot answer today (a file's name rarely appears inside another file; `search_files` greps content, `list_files` needs the dir already, `read_file` needs the path). Picked over Scout A's own higher-raw-value A3 `pla config` -- a 4th `cli.py` touch in seven iters (15/18/19), the layer-diversity headwind real and strengthening -- and over Scout B's RECOMMENDED B1 (registry-driven `TestGracefulDegradation`, #12): B1 is the highest-value hardening row but test-only, so it hits the dominant recurring failure mode -- test-only hardening starves the Engineer stage (parked iters 04/07/11/14-19) because the pipeline needs a PRODUCTION ARTIFACT -- whereas A1 ships a real capability AND a rich test surface. A1 is the convergence pick: new *executable* capability on the COLDEST productive layer (L1, 4 iters cold since iter-17 `append_file`), diversifying hard off the saturated L2-collector ground (16, 20) and the CLI-heavy 15/18/19 tail; it fills a VERIFIED gap (no current tool locates a file by name); and it is READ-ONLY, cleanly sidestepping the destructive-op veto that keeps `delete_file` parked. Verbatim reuse of every `search_files` guard (`_reject_unsafe`/`_within`/`_SKIP_DIRS`/`os.walk(followlinks=False)`) plus a stdlib `fnmatch.fnmatchcase(name.lower(), pattern.lower())` match (case-folded BOTH sides for cross-platform determinism) = low blast radius, richest zero-monkeypatch black-box surface, additive/no version bump, byte-stable demo. Scout B's B1 (#12), B3 (#19 sandbox symlink-parity suite) and B2 (#14 `py.typed`) remain the strongest hardening backlog next -- and B3 is now a natural fast-follow (a 6th path tool on the shared `_within` guard). See iter-21 pm.md.
 iter-22 ships `explain --json` (recorded as #29): a machine-readable form of the read-only, LLM-free autonomy-gate audit, mirroring the `--json` flag every other inspector already carries (`runs`/`trace`/`signals` + `scan --format json`). `explain` is the one read-only inspector with NO JSON form -- verified in source (`p_explain` at cli.py:192-199 adds only `--slate`/`--goal-id`; `_cmd_explain` prints only `_render_explain`) -- so the machine-readable-inspection thesis covers every stage of the transparency arc EXCEPT the gate-audit stage (signals -> scan -> [gap: explain] -> trace). Since `explain` audits the autonomy DECISION -- the product's headline safety readout -- closing this gap lets a script/CI assert on the gate itself ('fail if any sensitive goal resolved AUTO_DISPATCH'). Picked over Scout A's RECOMMENDED A1 `pla config` (net-new settings-inspector verb): both are cli.py, so layer-diversity does not separate them (CLI's last NEW verb was iter-18 `watch`; iters 19/20/21 went `_collect`-helper/L2/L1, so a CLI touch is non-saturating either way), but B1 wins on blast radius (a default-off FLAG on an existing verb + one pure payload helper + two doc lines, strictly smaller than A1's whole new subcommand + two render helpers) AND thesis-leverage (completes an existing cross-verb consistency gap on the highest-stakes readout vs adding net-new behavior). Ships a PRODUCTION ARTIFACT so it dodges the recurring test-only-starves-the-Engineer failure mode (#12/#19 still parked). Both scouts independently re-confirmed NO live docs-vs-code contradiction this iteration (SPEC S4.5 never claims `explain --json`), so the integrity tie-break stays dormant -- a straight value x safety x diversity call. The one real trap is the schema-leak: `--json` MUST be an explicit 12-key allowlist dict, NOT `model_dump()` (the iter-08 lesson), fully falsifiable via an exact-key-set test; human `explain` stays byte-stable; additive, no version bump; byte-stable demo. A1 `pla config` stays the top net-new backlog verb (twice-parked iters 20/21); Scout B's B2 (`make cov`) and B3 (provider-adapter README section) remain the strongest hardening backlog next. See iter-22 pm.md. iter-23 ships #30 (actionable "SDK not installed" error for the live providers): each live `_create_*` guards its lazy SDK import so a selected-but-uninstalled provider yields an `LLMError` with a `pip install <pkg>` / `--provider scripted` hint -- routed through iter-02's `main()` boundary to one `error:` line + exit 1 -- instead of a raw `ModuleNotFoundError` traceback. This closes that boundary's contract for the one foreseeable environment fault its narrow `except (LLMError, ValueError, OSError)` tuple deliberately excludes [`ImportError`], on the product's ONLY live-model surface, and honors `create_client`'s OWN asserted principle [providers.py:42] on the missing-SDK branch it never covered. Picked over Scout A's RECOMMENDED A1 `pla diff` [first comparative verb -- higher raw thesis-leverage, but a FRESH `cli.py` touch after iter-22 and Scout A's whole slate is cli.py/L1]: B1 lands on `providers.py`, the COLDEST runtime layer [1 commit], maximizing diversity off the CLI/L1/L2 tail; ships a PRODUCTION ARTIFACT [dodges the test-only-starves-the-Engineer mode]; fixes a real correctness gap a public-repo user hits first; rich zero-real-SDK black-box surface [`sys.modules[mod]=None`]. Guard scoped to the SDK import only, reuses the already-imported `LLMError`, scripted-no-leak invariant preserved, additive, no version bump, byte-stable demo. `pla diff` stays the top net-new backlog verb. See iter-23 pm.md. iter-24 returns to the queued backlog with #31 (`pla diff` -- the first comparative verb): the top net-new row deferred four straight iterations (20-23) on layer-diversity grounds that finally decayed -- the last NEW verb is iter-18 (six back) while 19-23 touched `_collect`/L2/L1/CLI-flag/providers, so a new verb is now the COLDEST CLI shape. It completes the namesake `watch` capability (a scan stream is only useful if you can diff it) and the read-only inspector family. Picked over Scout B's RECOMMENDED B1 `-v/--verbose` (recorded #32): both ship a production artifact, but the public-repo tie-break is blast radius x testability -- `diff` is a purely additive read-only verb (no existing path touched, no LLMClient, offline-falsifiable from two hand-written slate fixtures) whereas B1 puts PROCESS-GLOBAL logging config into a `main()` the 520-test suite calls repeatedly, with documented test-isolation traps. Load-bearing trap = match by NORMALIZED TITLE not the random `id`; additive, no version bump, byte-stable demo. See iter-24 pm.md. iter-25 ships #32 (`pla -v/-vv --verbose` runtime log verbosity): a `count`-action flag on the shared globals parent parser that, in `main()` before dispatch, maps count -> level (0->WARNING unchanged / 1->INFO / 2+->DEBUG) and configures the **package** logger (`proactive_loop`) once with a single sentinel-guarded `StreamHandler(sys.stderr)` [NOT bare `basicConfig`, NOT the root logger], plus one INFO line in `executor._count_retry` (`L0 retry N ...`) so the product's NAMESAKE 'resilient by design' story becomes visible at runtime -- iter-08 made retries visible AFTER a run (checkpoint data + summary/trace); this makes them visible DURING it (the only log-emit site in all of `src/` today is the iter-19 `_collect` WARNING; the executor's retry hook logged nothing). Picked over Scout A's RECOMMENDED A1 `CiConfigCollector` #34: A1 is new perception on the coldest productive layer and lead-accepted backlog, but on a PUBLIC portfolio repo its fatal flaw is the near-copy shape Scout A itself concedes -- 'the same structural shape as `TestPostureCollector`' (source dir + specific artifact absent -> flag), the exact shape that KILLED `ReadmeGapCollector` at iter-18 -- whose defense needs the reviewer to actively confirm the distinction (an irreducible subjective call), and which is a hygiene-trio completion the lead's own weighting rates BELOW genuinely-new perception. B1 instead lands on GENUINELY VIRGIN ground (runtime log verbosity untouched by every prior iter), serves the product's namesake thesis, ships a production artifact [dodging the test-only-starves-the-Engineer mode #12/#19], and its ONE real risk -- process-global logging config in a `main()` the 550-test suite calls repeatedly -- is a KNOWN pattern with a MECHANICALLY-VERIFIABLE fix (level-0 strict no-op keeps the suite byte-stable; sentinel-guarded single handler is directly assertable as <=1; caplog captures the executor INFO record independent of handler config, the proven iter-19 test mechanism) rather than A1's subjective near-copy judgment. Over B2 `make cov` [dev-tooling, thin product-behavior surface for the isolated Tester] and B3 symlink suite [test-only, deferred 5x iters 04/07/11/14-21]. SEQUENCING: iter-24 recorded B1 as #32, 'the STRONGEST deferred alternative,' deferred behind `diff` ONLY on comparative blast radius 'not on merit'; `diff` shipped iter-24 so that reason decayed and the slot is open. Load-bearing traps for the Engineer: level-0 = strict no-op (attach nothing), single sentinel-guarded stderr handler on the `proactive_loop` package logger (never `basicConfig`/root), stderr-only so `--json` stdout stays pipe-clean, executor INFO emitted unconditionally at source. Additive, no version bump [0.1.1]; byte-stable demo [`make demo` passes no `-v`]. See iter-25 pm.md. iter-32 ships #38 (a net-new **`ollama` local-model provider** in `llm/providers.py`) -- the FIRST offline runtime backend, extending the product's offline-first thesis from the `scripted` test double to real plan->act->check execution against a locally-hosted model (no API key, no network egress). Picked over BOTH scouts' RECOMMENDED picks and Scout A's own top pick: over B1 write-side symlink suite [test-only -> would land test-only BACK-TO-BACK with iter-31, the most-documented failure mode 'test-only starves the Engineer stage'; B1 is decay-free so it loses nothing by waiting -> natural fast-follow], over A1 `pla stats` [iter-30 already rejected it ON MERIT as 'a 12th read-only CLI inspector on the most-worked layer = verb-sprawl on a mature repo' -- CLI is the HOTTEST layer (15/18/19/22/24/25/27) while providers is the COLDEST productive layer (~9 iters cold since iter-23, which added only the missing-SDK GUARD -- this adds a real new BACKEND)], over A2 `secret_leak` collector [highest raw value but a real PUBLIC-repo second-order hazard: fixtures/regex literals can trip external secret scanners on this repo -- disqualifying on the 'a bad commit is publicly visible' bar; L2 also saturated], and over B2 `make cov` [thin Tester surface, dead-dep is not a docs-vs-code contradiction so the integrity tie-break stays dormant] / B3 `--json` purity [test-only, re-proves a per-verb-covered invariant]. The convergence pick: ships a PRODUCTION ARTIFACT (dodges the test-only-starves-the-Engineer mode) on the COLDEST productive layer (max diversity off the CLI(27)->L2(28)->L1(29)->config(30)->L2-test(31) tail), serves the product's CORE offline-first identity at RUNTIME, and carries a rich FULLY-OFFLINE black-box Tester surface via the exact iter-23 mechanism [`monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules,'ollama',None)` + a self-contained stub module -- zero real SDK/network/keys]. Reuses `_require` + `_SdkAdapter` verbatim; the load-bearing constraint = source BOTH throttle/timeout exception tuples from the `ollama` namespace ONLY (`ResponseError`/`RequestError`, no `httpx` import) so the present-SDK path is constructible from one stub. `ollama` stays an OPTIONAL lazily-imported SDK (pydantic remains the only runtime dep, SPEC S5); additive SPEC S4.2 + README edits, no version bump [0.1.1], byte-stable demo [scripted default untouched]. Scout B's B1 write-side symlink suite [#19] remains the strongest hardening backlog next; A1 `pla stats` stays the top net-new CLI backlog row (but re-judge the verb-sprawl concern on a mature repo). See iter-32 pm.md. iter-60 ships #61 (wire the L2 synthesizer's `on_retry` logging hook): the `GoalLoop` executor emits a live `L0 retry N for <tag>` INFO record per recovered PLAN/CHECK throttle (iters 08/25), but the L2 scout's front-door call `synthesizer.synthesize()` passed NO `on_retry` into its `with_retry(...)` -- the ONE unset call site in `src/` -- so a recovered throttle on `pla scan`/`pla run` (the verbs a user hits FIRST) was absorbed SILENTLY, even though `synthesize()`'s docstring promises "resilience parity with the L1 loop." Picked over Scout A's RECOMMENDED A1 (`diff_files` read-only unified-diff L1 tool): both ship a production artifact (dodging the test-only-starves-the-Engineer mode after the iter-58 packaging / iter-59 test-pin tail), but B1 wins on all three deciding axes on a PUBLIC repo -- (1) LOWEST blast radius (ONE `src/` file, a verbatim mirror of the shipped/tested `executor._count_retry` pattern, vs A1's tool-catalog + `pla tools` drift-guard + tool-count fixture + PLAN-prompt line + truncation/binary edges); (2) diversifies HARDER (the synthesizer retry-observability seam has NEVER been wired -- the only log-emit sites are executor 08/25 and `_collect` 19 -- vs A1 being another L1 read tool right after iter-54 `tail_file`); (3) serves the headline resilience thesis at the layer users hit first + closes a docs-vs-code parity gap. Log-ONLY (no `RunState` in the scan path, so no retries counter -- the one deliberate difference from the executor); additive, no version bump [0.1.1], byte-stable demo (scripted path never throttles). A1 stays the top net-new L1 backlog row. See iter-60 pm.md.

| 4 | GitHub Actions CI (`uv sync` + `uv run pytest` + `make demo`) + a "tests passing" README badge — first trust signal on a public repo; machine-verifies every future loop commit | DX/CI | High | Very Low | Scout B C2 (iter-02) | **SHIPPED — human, out-of-band (2026-08-04)**: added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (matrix Python 3.12/3.13: `uv sync --locked` → `uv run pytest` → `make demo` → assert the demo wrote a slate + artifacts, so CI proves BOTH halves of the offline claim); swapped the rotting hardcoded `tests-NNNN-passing` shields badge for the live Actions badge; converted the two exact “1,872 tests” intro claims to a monotonically-safe “1,800+” floor; and added `tests/test_readme_and_ci_contract.py` (5 tests) binding the intro’s collector/verb counts to the live registries and the workflow to its three documented commands. The human-owned marker gained a NARROW numbers-only carve-out so that guard forces a fix instead of deadlocking the loop. Scouts: do NOT re-propose. (Historical deferral rationale: re-proposed ~11× since iter-02 and deferred every time for a STRUCTURAL reason, not neglect: (a) the isolated black-box Tester tests `pla`-CLI/public-API behaviors, and a CI workflow file exposes NONE — only "the YAML parses" — see #5's iter-09 note "exposes no black-box behavior for the tester stage"; (b) a workflow's green/red is unverifiable until it runs on GitHub, so a misconfigured first-run red-X would sit publicly UNDER the static "tests passing" badge = a NET credibility loss the loop cannot pre-empt; (c) the real win — swapping the static badge for a live CI badge — sits ABOVE the human-owned README `PORTFOLIO INTRO` marker. So CI is a HUMAN task (author `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, confirm the first run is green on GitHub, then swap the badge), NOT a loop iteration. ENHANCEMENT: `README.md` gains one `## Project documents` section BELOW the human-owned portfolio marker, linking `SPEC.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md` and `DIRECTIONS.md` with the audience for each, plus `tests/test_iter189_behavior.py`, whose domain is DERIVED from `git ls-files "*.md"` so the next companion document cannot be added unlinked. So ~527KB of committed design contract, live backlog, archive and per-iteration decision log was reachable only by browsing the file tree and guessing, and the reference traffic ran one way (`ROADMAP.md` links down to `SPEC.md` and the archive; nothing linked back up to the reader). WHY THE GUARD IS THE POINT: without it this is a one-off docs edit that rots on the next companion, which is exactly how the finding was lost twice -- raised as iter-184 scout B3, re-derived from scratch as iter-185 scout A1, and rowed neither time (`ROADMAP.md` held 0 occurrences of `DIRECTIONS`). WHY THE DOMAIN IS GIT AND NOT A GLOB OR A PINNED TUPLE: two root-level Markdown files on the authoring machine (`README_TOP.md`, `LINKEDIN_POST.md`, both wildly stale) are excluded via `.git/info/exclude:11-12`, a per-clone UNCOMMITTED mechanism, so a glob domain would red the build on exactly one machine (the 2026-08-11 operator lesson), while a pinned 4-tuple would pass green the day a fifth companion lands unlinked. DEGRADE CONTRACT INHERITED, NOT INVENTED: the module imports `root_markdown_names` / `tracked_root_markdown` from `tests/test_iter133_behavior.py` rather than writing a second parser that can disagree, and keeps that helper's settled SKIP where git cannot answer (a tarball export has no index) instead of adding a contradictory fail-hard rule for the same condition; the vacuity worry is answered on the other side, as an assertion that the derived domain is non-empty and >= 4 members, the shape `test_iter144_behavior.py` already uses. TWO-SIDED PROOF: the checker `unlinked_companions(readme_text, domain)` is pure and total, and is fired at synthetic READMEs -- 3 of 4 linked returns exactly the missing name, all 4 returns `[]`, a link ABOVE the marker does NOT count (which is what makes the below-marker clause enforceable rather than decorative), `](ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md)` does not satisfy `ROADMAP.md`, and a text with NO marker raises `ValueError` instead of treating offset 0 as below the marker. LAYER: published surface + test integrity. VALUE: High on a PUBLIC portfolio repo -- `DIRECTIONS.md` is 71,171 chars over 93 iteration blocks recording what the loop considered, rejected and shipped, which is the differentiator a recruiter link should reach in one click. RISK: Very Low -- zero `src/` lines, no new verb, flag or collector, therefore no `SPEC.md` 4.1/4.5 edit and no count above the human-owned marker; the frozen intro is byte-identical. - **#224 -- `pla scan` has no `--json` flag, so the machine-readable idiom is 15/16 verbs and `scan` is the odd one out** (created and retired in the SAME iteration, iter-187, factory iter 190, so it never occupied the live index -- recorded here because the PM must land its record in the shipping commit, and a settled row parked in the index would trip the retire-on-ship brake). Fields labelled rather than piped so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row. ENHANCEMENT: `scan` gains `--json` as a PARSE-TIME ALIAS for the already-shipped `--format json`, spelled `action="store_const", const="json", dest="format"` inside a TWO-member mutually exclusive group with `--format`, so `args.format == "json"` flows through the existing render path and ZERO rendering or downstream code changes. WHY THE REGISTRATION ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: `--format` is added FIRST so its `default="table"` survives -- argparse seeds the namespace from the FIRST action that names a dest, and a `store_const` has no default of its own, so reversing the two lines would silently make a bare `scan` render nothing. TWO MEASUREMENT CORRECTIONS made downstream of the spec rather than inherited. (a) The spec's acceptance wording "byte-identical" is LITERALLY FALSE for a CORRECT implementation: a slate carries a wall-clock `created_at` and every goal id is `uuid4().hex[:12]` (`models.py:44`, already documented in `cli.py` as "the random per-scan id"), so a raw comparison reds a correct build. The control that proved it is one command -- `--format json` against ITSELF differed in exactly the same two fields -- and the oracle therefore runs under the suite's existing `_HEX12` normaliser (already shipped in 5 behavior modules), plus a pin that the normaliser cannot erase real content, i.e. the table view must stay distinguishable from the JSON view under it. (b) The spec's design-proof census "NO test introspects `_actions` ... 0 hits across `tests/`" is FALSE -- `_actions`/`option_strings` occur in 10+ test modules -- and it is MOOT for a reason that does not depend on the count: `_MutuallyExclusiveGroup._add_action` delegates to `self._container._add_action`, so both options still land in the parser's `_optionals` and every `_actions` walker sees them unchanged. Help ORDER is preserved; the only visible change is the usage line, which now shows the two options as one exclusive pair rather than two independent optionals. Prefer the mechanism argument, not the census. NO README EDIT IS REQUIRED AND NONE WAS MADE, verified against the guard's own domain rather than by eye: `flag_universe` (`tests/test_readme_and_ci_contract.py:225`) UNIONS the root parser with every subparser, so `--json` was already inside the guarded universe and the README `## CLI` section already documents it. The human-owned portfolio intro is byte-identical and no published count moved. ORACLE: `tests/test_iter190_behavior.py`, 14 tests, all black-box (argv in -> exit code + stdout/stderr + on-disk artifacts out). The module name is DERIVED FROM THE REPO, never from the state-dir counter, which is the defect that cost iter-186 a shipped oracle: highest tracked `test_iterNN_behavior.py` was 189, +1 = 190, `git cat-file -e HEAD:tests/test_iter190_behavior.py` FAILED (path provably free in HEAD) and the worktree path was absent. TWO-SIDED PROOF, run against a HEAD tree materialised read-only via `git archive HEAD | tar -x` outside the live repo, with `proactive_loop.__file__` PRINTED FIRST to prove which module was imported (had the editable install won over PYTHONPATH, the control would have silently re-tested the patched source and "passed"): 10 FAILED with verbatim `unrecognized arguments: --json` at rc=2 -- every new-behavior test -- and 4 PASSED, precisely the tests that encode UNCHANGED behavior. All 14 failing would have meant a broken fixture, so the split is what makes the 10 attributable to the feature's absence. One ordering claim was upgraded into evidence for a single cheap check: for `scan --json --format csv` the gate asserts not only exit 2 and empty stdout but that the `--state-dir` was NEVER CREATED, which proves the parse-time rejection precedes provider construction and collection rather than merely preceding rendering -- an exit code alone cannot distinguish those two orderings. DELIBERATELY NOT INCLUDED: `watch --json`, which is why the idiom lands at 15/16 and not 16/16 -- `watch` has no machine-readable mode at all and a deterministic offline oracle for it needs `--max-scans` plus the scripted provider, which does not fit an `engineer` stage measured at a 600.0s median with 0.0s headroom. Record it as the remaining gap. Also excluded: deprecating, deleting or rewording `--format`, whose five choices are unchanged and which stays the general form. LAYER: L3/CLI surface. VALUE: Med (one idiom across the perception verb every adoption path starts at). RISK: Very Low (8 lines in one subparser, no `src/` logic path, no new rendering branch). SOURCE: PM scout-B B2 (iter-187), triaged ahead of scout-A A1 (compress `ROADMAP.md`) after the PM RE-MEASURED A1's premise and found it priced the wrong artifact -- A1 costed the mean QUEUED backlog row at 962 chars against 1,127 chars of headroom, but the record a shipping PM is actually required to add is one terse Done-ledger line, measured at avg 91 chars and 111 in the most recent ship, with the per-commit `ROADMAP.md` delta over the last 12 touching commits running +111, +931, +510, -991, +694, +1430, +120, +114, -1308, -2123, +154, +1141, i.e. a median of +332 and three NEGATIVE, because retiring settled rows is the file's own self-correction. The file has already sat at 39,385 chars (iter-179, 615 chars of headroom) and came back down without an operator. STATUS: SHIPPED -- iter-187, factory iter 190. - **#225 -- Nothing states what ONE `pla scan` is allowed to COST in units of work: every throughput property this product has won is protected either as dated prose or per-feature** (created and retired in the SAME iteration, iter-189, factory iter 191, so it never occupied the live index -- recorded here because the PM must land its record in the shipping commit, and a settled row parked in the index would trip the retire-on-ship brake). Fields labelled rather than piped so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row. ENHANCEMENT: one new oracle module, `tests/test_iter192_behavior.py` (482 lines, 13 tests, ZERO `src/` lines and zero new dependencies, so `uv.lock` is untouched), pins in COUNTS rather than durations what a single scan may spend: physical directory traversals, source compilations and child processes. WHY COUNTS AND NEVER DURATIONS: counts are deterministic and immune to a loaded shared runner, whereas a millisecond threshold reds a PUBLIC portfolio build for a reason no contributor caused; the technique was already proven here once by iter-187's counting `os.walk` wrapper, so this generalises a working pattern rather than inventing one. WHY EVERY BUDGET IS A CEILING (`<=`) AND NEVER AN EQUALITY: scout B correctly objected that pinning today's traversal count RATCHETS IN TODAY'S WASTE and reads as approval of it. A `<=` ceiling cannot bless waste -- it forbids only getting WORSE, it goes GREEN rather than RED the day a collector is converted onto `dir_source.walk`, and it therefore never becomes a coupled edit a future iteration has to fight; each failure message names the TARGET verbatim and forbids raising the constant. SIX BEHAVIORS, all against a tree the test CREATES under `tmp_path`: (1) fixture isolation is ASSERTED, not assumed -- the scan root is proved to be inside `tmp_path` and NOT the repository root, because the 08-11 learning is explicit that a count asserted against the ambient working tree passes only on this machine and breaks in the fresh-clone preship; (2) `os.walk` calls over one `cli._collect(root)` are `<= WALK_BUDGET`; (3) the shared provider performs EXACTLY one physical traversal per scope -- `dir_source.walk_cache_stats()["misses"] == 1` with `["hits"] >= 1`, an equality on `misses` because one walk per scope is the provider's correctness contract rather than a budget with slack; (4) no source file is compiled twice, `builtins.compile` calls `<= PARSES_PER_SOURCE_FILE` per distinct unpruned `.py` file, with the failure message naming `syntax_error._PARSE_MEMO` as the mechanism being protected and the fixture carrying a syntactically INVALID file so the count is non-trivial; (5) `subprocess.run` calls are `<= CHILD_PROCESS_BUDGET` (2, the two shipped git invocations) AND no two calls share an identical argv -- the duplicate-free half is the load-bearing one because it holds with or without a `.git` directory; (6) the oracle is TWO-SIDED -- each counting shim is proved to actually count by invoking the wrapped callable a known number of times, because a shim that silently records zero would make behaviors 2, 4 and 5 vacuously green, which is the fail-open shape this repo has been burned by. Every monkeypatch is installed and removed by a fixture with unconditional `try/finally` teardown, and the three real callables are captured ONCE at import before any rebind, so a shim cannot recurse into itself. `WALK_BUDGET = 10` IS THE FIXTURE-OBSERVED COUNT AND WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: the reviewer left this as its one undecided item, and the fix pass settled it by forcing the ceiling to 1 and reading the assertion's own failure text, which reported `10 physical traversals of one root` -- so the constant is exactly what `pm.md` Behavior 2 requires (the count observed on this fixture) and carries no unauthorised slack; 1 remains the correct number and the message says so. Note the ambient repo scan performs 8 such traversals while this fixture performs 10, which is itself the argument for a fixture-local count: the ambient figure is not reproducible in a fresh clone. A2 WAS TRIED FIRST AND ITS PREMISE WAS FALSIFIED (recorded so no future scout re-sells it): deleting the duplicated `_SKIP_DIRS`/`_is_hidden` copies in `todos` and `notes` was the better-argued candidate, and its headline claim -- that `todos` content-scans a `.tox` tree its ten siblings prune -- measured as 0 signals, because `.tox` starts with a dot and `_is_hidden` prunes it before `_SKIP_DIRS` is ever consulted; all 11 real prune sites in `src/` apply BOTH predicates, so three of the seven `_SKIP_DIRS` members are redundant at every call site and the 6-vs-7 divergence produces no output difference. That reduces A2 to a cosmetic dedup inside `src/`, which is the worst trade available against an `engineer` stage measured at a 600.0s median with 0.0s headroom -- the single strongest input to this triage and the reason the pick has zero `src/` lines. PROCESS RECORD, kept because it is the honest history of this commit: the `engineer` stage was CAP-KILLED at 600s (48-byte attempt log) after writing the oracle and BEFORE touching either roadmap file, and the `reviewer` stage was cap-killed too, leaving a checkpoint whose single `[BLOCKING]` finding was precisely this missing PM record; the fix pass landed it. MODULE NUMBER AND COMMIT TAG ARE NOW TWO DIFFERENT COUNTERS, and this record corrects a conflation: the oracle is `test_iter192_behavior.py` because the module number is DERIVED FROM THE REPO (highest tracked `test_iterNN` is 191, +1 = 192, and `git cat-file -e HEAD:tests/test_iter192_behavior.py` FAILED, proving the path free), whereas the commit tag is factory iter **191** = the highest `factory iter N` in `git log` (190) + 1. The two decoupled at state-iter 188, which added `test_iter191_behavior.py` as a second oracle for factory iteration 190. This iteration's `engineer.md` and `reviewer.md` headers both say `factory iter 192`, inheriting the module number; that is wrong, and it is wrong from ONE shared source rather than from three independent readings. Measured instead: 186 commits carry dense tags 1..190 with NO duplicates and exactly four gaps (43, 105, 121, 160, each a reverted iteration), and the mapping is confirmed three ways -- state-185 shipped as 189, state-187 as 190, and state-188's re-land as 190 again. LAYER: L2/throughput integrity. VALUE: Med-High (one place says what a scan costs, so the next collector that hand-rolls a traversal or re-parses a file reds the build instead of silently costing milliseconds). RISK: Very Low (one new test file, zero `src/` lines, no new dependency, no rendering or CLI surface touched). SOURCE: PM scout-B B3 (iter-189), with scout B's ratchet objection resolved by the PM rather than deferred. STATUS: SHIPPED -- iter-189, factory iter 191.

NOT purely behavior-preserving -- the `.tox` case changes for both collectors, so it needs one assertion each -- and `test_iter109_behavior.py` enumerates the local 6-member set as "the first six", so that comment is repaired in the same commit.. LAYER: Hygiene/DX. VALUE: Med. RISK: Low. SOURCE: PM scout-B B1 (iter-146). PRIOR STATUS AT SELECTION: **QUEUED -- now the SOLE owner of this item; row #118 was merged in and retired in iter-156** (its cosmetic/INERT caveat, falsified three times, is preserved verbatim in the archive). It is a GUARD, not a fix: `_is_hidden` is `startswith(".")` and every prune site ANDs it, so `.tox` is already pruned and output is byte-identical. Honest residual: ~10 deleted lines plus an `ast` single-definition drift guard. Never pitch it as a `.tox` fix -- such a behavior test passes VACUOUSLY. OUTCOME: **SHIPPED -- iter-190** (factory iter 192). Both holdouts now `from proactive_loop.collectors.filesystem import _SKIP_DIRS, _is_hidden`, so the collectors package holds exactly ONE definition of each -- measured after the edit by an `ast` census over `src/proactive_loop/collectors/*.py` matching BOTH `ast.AnnAssign` and `ast.Assign` targets (all three former sites were ANNOTATED, so an `Assign`-only matcher finds ZERO): `_SKIP_DIRS` sites = `['filesystem.py']`, `_is_hidden` sites = `['filesystem.py']`. The proof is non-vacuous by IDENTITY, not equality (`todos._SKIP_DIRS is filesystem._SKIP_DIRS`, and both `_is_hidden` are the same function object), mirroring the iter-153 `_has_source` hoist whose docstring names the vacuous-proof trap. Output is byte-identical as row #178 always said it would be: the two collectors GAIN `.tox`, which every prune site already dropped because it ANDs `_is_hidden` and `.tox` is dot-prefixed -- so this shipped as the GUARD it was priced as, never as a `.tox` fix. THE REAL VALUE, and it is why the row outranked a net-new capability on this iteration's tie-break: `collectors/dir_source.py` justifies serving ONE shared directory listing to every walking collector on the written claim that the product has "exactly ONE prune set and there is no set-difference risk to reconcile", and that claim was FALSE on the tree (`filesystem` 7 members, `todos` 6, `notes` 6, symmetric difference `{.tox}` in both cases). Deleting the copies makes the existing paragraph TRUE rather than weakening the prose to match drifted code, so `dir_source.py` needed NO edit. The first NON-dotted addition to `_SKIP_DIRS` (`vendor`, `target`, `htmlcov`) would have silently split the package's prune policy in two under a docstring asserting that cannot happen. `filesystem.py` is unchanged, `large_file.py` keeps its import (it calls `_is_hidden` on FILES), and `test_iter109_behavior.py`'s "the first six are the members of the skip contract the module already declares" comment was repaired in the same commit per this row's own coupling note. UNBLOCKS row #210's remaining `notes`/`todos` walk conversions, which that row records as blocked on precisely this divergence.

WHY THE ROSTER CONTRACTS ARE EMPTY-OR-COMPLETE: the defect was never 'no collectors listed', it was '4 of 17 listed', which READS as complete -- so naming none is honest, naming all is honest, and only the misleading middle fails, which also means the guard never forces a future author to keep the elision style. TWO-SIDED PROOF, run against `HEAD:SPEC.md` in-process rather than only against synthetic samples: on the pre-fix fence the collector-count and verb-count contracts BOTH raise ('found 0' declarations), the collector roster check returns the 13 omitted modules, the cli.py line check returns exactly {config, verify} and its cites-4.5 clause is False, the collectors-4.1 routing line does not exist, and the retired parity claim is present -- 6 of 7 content behaviors red on the file as shipped, so the guard is known to FIRE and not merely known to pass. Three synthetic negative controls ship with it (count one lower, the 14-verb line, the 4-module roster), each derived from the LIVE fence by one surgical edit so a firing control is attributable to that edit. HONEST RESIDUAL, recorded so no reviewer has to find it: behavior 7's 'the tests/ line carries no digit' clause was ALREADY satisfied before this commit, so it is a FORWARD-LOOKING ban on reintroducing a per-iteration-churning count, not the fix of a live defect; the live defect on that line was the false 'one test module per package' parity claim, which is retired and is now banned by literal. CASE SENSITIVITY IS LOAD-BEARING AND PINNED: the fence carries `LICENSE` for the MIT file, which must not be read as the `license` collector, so the roster match is case-sensitive and word-boundaried and has its own test. MODULE NAMING SIDESTEPS THE ITER-186 COLLISION CLASS ENTIRELY: the file is named for its subject (`test_spec_layout_contract.py`, mirroring `test_spec_contract.py`) rather than from any iteration counter, and `git cat-file -e HEAD:tests/test_spec_layout_contract.py` FAILED, proving the path free in HEAD before it was written. - **#136 -- Oracle the 2 unguarded `_out_dir_guard` messages** (SHIPPED in iter-192, factory iter 194, and retired from the live index in the same commit; it had occupied the index since iter-116). Verbatim index text as it stood at retirement, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **Oracle the 2 unguarded `_out_dir_guard` messages** -- measured iter-116: no test file contains `--out-dir is not a directory` or `--out-dir parent`, and no test names `_out_dir_guard`, so the NEWEST of the 3 CLI path guards (row #132) is the least guarded while `test_iter44_behavior.py` pins the other 3 messages verbatim. LAYER: CLI/test. VALUE: Low-Med. RISK: Very Low. SOURCE: PM scout-B B1 (iter-116), separated from its guard-collapse by PM-lead. PRIOR STATUS AT SELECTION: **QUEUED**. OUTCOME: **SHIPPED -- iter-192** (factory iter 194) as `tests/test_iter196_behavior.py`, a 13-test TWO-SIDED oracle driving `watch` as a black box through `main(argv)`. The premise was re-measured before writing and held: both rejection strings and the helper name returned 0 hits across `git ls-files tests`, against a CONTROL of 17 hits for the shared substring `is not a directory` (the three sibling guards), so the zero came from an absent oracle rather than a broken matcher. REJECT half: an `--out-dir` that IS an existing plain file, and an existing plain file in the PARENT CHAIN, each pinned to one exact `error:` line under the sibling guards' full invariant (exit 2, empty stdout, stderr exactly one line, no `[Errno`, no `Traceback`); the parent case asserts the message names the deepest EXISTING ancestor by requiring the longer literal parent to be ABSENT, because `str(F)` is a PREFIX of `str(F/'sub')` and the presence direction would also pass for the wrong message. ACCEPT half (the fail-closed side, which reds if a future edit widens the guard): an all-new nested `D/a/b/c` exits 0, is created, and holds exactly one `slate-001.json`. MEASURED TRAP now pinned by a precondition test: without the bundled offline driver the tick FAILS while `watch` still exits 0 by design, creating no directory and no slate, so an accept-path test written without it asserts almost nothing. Guard ORDER is pinned too -- with a missing workspace AND a file `--out-dir`, only the workspace message appears. NON-VACUITY was PROBED PER ASSERTION at the fix-review gate, and the honest result is PARTIAL rather than total: neutering `_out_dir_guard` in memory under the module's OWN reject argv flips exit 2 -> 0, prints the `=== scan 1 ===` header, and breaks the stderr-equality clause (stderr carries the tick's own `scan 1 failed: provider is 'scripted' ...` line in place of the expected `error:` line), so 3 of `_assert_clean_rejection`'s 5 assertions fire. The `no [Errno` clause does NOT fire under that argv, because without `--scripted-responses` the tick dies on the PROVIDER before any write -- the module's own trap 1 turned against its own proof; APPENDING the driver does fire it, and with a DIFFERENT errno per case (`[Errno 17] File exists` for the file case, `[Errno 20] Not a directory` for the parent-chain case, both named by the guard's docstring). The `no Traceback` clause fires in NEITHER variant, because the per-tick handler prints a one-line `scan 1 failed: ...` and never a traceback. So those two clauses ship as forward-looking guard-rails inherited from the sibling guards' oracle, NOT as mutation-proven assertions -- recorded because on this repo a trusted fail-open check is worse than no check, and the original wording of this bullet claimed all four halves fired when 3 of 5 do. ZERO `src/` edits: this row shipped an oracle for already-correct behavior.

- **#227 -- Both graded gates assert the demo's artifacts EXIST and read its slate back, but NOTHING anywhere runs the `run --json` document the README sells as the scriptable surface -- and `examples/check_run.py`, written to BE that proof, has zero callers outside `tests/`** (created and retired in the SAME iteration, iter-193, factory iter 195, so it never occupied the live index -- recorded here because the PM must land its record in the shipping commit, and a settled row parked in the index would trip the retire-on-ship brake). Fields labelled rather than piped so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row. ENHANCEMENT: the `demo` recipe becomes exactly THREE steps -- `mkdir -p .pla_runs`, the pre-existing `uv run pla run ...` invocation extended with one `--json` and redirected into `.pla_runs/run.json`, then `uv run python examples/check_run.py < .pla_runs/run.json` -- so `make check` and CI EXECUTE the published JSON contract instead of only asserting that files exist. MEASURED AT THE ENGINEER GATE, not inherited from the slate: `make demo` exits 0 and its last line is `ok: run_id=773ea3ddf5a6 status=done iterations=3 llm_calls=6 artifacts=2`; the joined recipe holds 3 steps, exactly one `--snapshot` valued `.pla_runs/snapshot.json`, one `--json`, no `>>`, and its only `--state-dir` value is `.pla_runs`, which is what keeps `test_iter186_behavior.py::test_b1` (the `--state-dir .pla_runs --snapshot .pla_runs/snapshot.json` adjacency substring) and `test_iter110_behavior.py::test_b4_demo_recipe_passes_the_same_state_dir` green -- the new flag is APPENDED AFTER `--snapshot` for exactly that reason. WHY A FILE AND NOT A PIPE: a pipeline reports only its LAST command's status, so a `pla run` that DIED would be graded as the consumer's exit 2 ("stdin is not one JSON document") -- a true failure reported under a false cause. Two separate recipe steps let `make` grade `pla run` itself, and the document stays an inspectable artifact. WHY `mkdir -p .pla_runs` LEADS: the shell opens the `>` redirect BEFORE `pla run` starts and `make check` opens with `rm -rf .pla_runs`, so without it the gate would die on a missing directory rather than on anything the demo did; the redirect is a single truncating `>` so the graded document is always THIS run's and can never be graded stale. WHY NO NEW GATE STEP: `check` composes `$(MAKE) demo` and CI runs `make demo`, so grading INSIDE the recipe reaches BOTH graded gates with `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the `check` recipe, `test_iter110`'s 6-element `expensive` list and `test_iter102`'s `CI_GATE_STEPS` / `EXPECTED_CI_RUN_STEPS` all UNTOUCHED -- the 3-site byte-identical pin coupling was priced and deliberately avoided against an engineer stage measured at a 600.0s median with 0.0s headroom. WHY THE REDIRECT IS DOCUMENTED IN PROSE AND NEVER INSIDE THE FENCE: `test_iter116_behavior.py` `shlex.split`s any fenced logical line whose first token is `pla` (or which starts `uv run pla`) and hands EVERY token to the live parser, so a `>` becomes argv, argparse exits and `SystemExit` reds the build; the README fence therefore gains only `--json` while the redirect is stated in the surrounding sentence, and the new module pins that as a DERIVED guard so the trap is enforced rather than remembered. LAYER: build and documentation only -- Makefile recipe plus README prose BELOW the human-owned portfolio-intro marker. ZERO `src/` lines, no new CLI flag, no dependency change, so `uv.lock` is untouched and CI's `uv sync --locked` cannot drift. VALUE: this is the third instance of one pattern this repo has already paid twice to close -- the armed signals self-scan (iter 128) and `verify --fail-on-unresolved` (iter 180) both existed to end an advertised-but-never-demonstrated integration, and the consumer written to prove the JSON contract had become an instance of the same defect itself. The gates now run the exact script a stranger would copy, and because that consumer imports its success value from `proactive_loop.models.RunStatus` rather than typing `"done"`, renaming that enum member reds a PUBLIC build instead of silently reporting failure on every successful run. RISK: low and bounded to the gate -- one recipe, no runtime code. The two failure modes considered were a redirect into a wiped directory (closed by the leading `mkdir -p`) and a fail-open green (closed by proving the consumer BOTH accepts the demo's real document and returns exit 1 when `dispatched.status` is not `done`, from a single in-process run). SOURCE: PM scout-B B1 (iter-193). PRIOR STATUS AT SELECTION: n/a -- created and shipped in the same iteration. OUTCOME: SHIPPED. `make demo` grades its own run through a committed consumer on every push and in every local `make check`.

- **#228 -- `README.md:437-440` publishes the join "`run_id`/`status` matching that run dir's `checkpoint.json`" and NOTHING executes it: the `run --json` document is built in memory from `RunState` and never read back, both graded gates check the run directory by EXISTENCE only (`test -f .pla_runs/slate.json`, `ls .pla_runs/run-*/artifacts/*.md`), and iter 193 (row #227) deliberately graded only the STDOUT half -- so a demo that persisted a truncated, empty or contradictory `checkpoint.json` passes every graded step while SPEC's "resilient by design" thesis rests entirely on that file.** SHIPPED in iter-194, factory iter 196, created and retired in the same commit. Shape: `examples/check_run.py` (the consumer the `demo` recipe already invokes, so NO new gate step and the four pinned gate-contract sites stay byte-identical) gains one pure comparison function plus IO in `main()`, cross-checking exactly the two fields the README names; every checkpoint failure -- missing, unreadable, unparseable, or disagreeing -- reuses exit **1** rather than inventing a code 3, because the docstring justifies exactly three codes and an absent or contradictory checkpoint means the run did not verifiably succeed. The summary line additionally reports `run_dir=`, the value `resume --run-dir` accepts, while KEEPING `run_id=`: scout B falsified the premise that `state.run_id` names nothing by measuring `dispatched.run_id` == `run-*/checkpoint.json`'s `run_id`, so the field is the checkpoint's identity rather than a vestigial id, and `test_iter198:427-429` pins that the summary names it. Deferred on purpose against an engineer stage at a 600.0s median with 0.0s headroom: the counter cross-checks (`iterations_used`, `llm_calls_used`, `retries`, `parse_errors`), `meta.json` (carries no `run_id`, and row #151 owns its corruption-tolerance), and every A1 direction that renames or adds a published key (13 test modules plus 6 README sites).

- **#229 -- The aggregated absorbed-manifest WARNING shipped in factory iter 184 as TWO hand-copied methods whose executable bodies are byte-identical, and it has ZERO test coverage, so the diagnostic is fail-open: `collectors/dependencies.py:103-141` and `collectors/lockfile_drift.py:124-162` are 39 lines each (an early `return` on empty, then one `_LOG.warning` with the same format string and the same three arguments `self.name`, `len(absorbed)`, `min(absorbed)`), `_log_absorbed` appears 0 times under `tests/`, and the three test modules that name these collectors do so only in import statements -- so the message could lose its count, lose its path, change its logger or vanish entirely and the suite would stay green.** SHIPPED in iter-195, factory iter 197, created and retired in the same commit; fields are labelled rather than piped so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row. ENHANCEMENT: build the missing oracle FIRST (a caplog module pinning, for BOTH collectors, exactly one WARNING record naming the collector's `name`, the absorbed count and the lexicographically smallest absorbed relative path, with a two-sided silent case, an aggregation case at N>=3 asserted against a test-computed `min()` rather than a hardcoded filename, and the emitted-before-the-cap case that the shipped docstring claims and nothing checked), then delete both methods and host ONE on `BaseCollector` as a plain method. THE DRIFT THIS CLOSES IS PROSE, NOT ONLY LINES: the two ~31-line docstrings documenting one shared contract had already diverged in two places -- one says `max_manifests` and "read as 'no dependency signals'", the other says `max_items` and "read as 'no drift'" -- so the surviving docstring is deliberately GENERIC by the `_relative` precedent and names neither field. WHY THE MOVE IS CHEAP: both classes already subclass `BaseCollector` (`dependencies.py:51`, `lockfile_drift.py:65`) and both already call `self._log_absorbed(absorbed)` at exactly one site each (`dependencies.py:96`, `lockfile_drift.py:117`), so there are ZERO call-site edits, and `BaseCollector` already hosts `collect`, `_collect` and `_relative` with a docstring that invites exactly this. PM DECISION, handed up by the scout and settled here rather than deferred: the hoisted record rides `BaseCollector`'s module logger, so it moves from each collector's own module logger to `proactive_loop.collectors.base` -- accepted, not worked around, because (1) it needs no parameter, no `type(self).__module__` lookup and no branch, (2) it makes the shipped docstring's own argument literally true, since that prose reasons about an operator filtering "this logger" also suppressing "the boundary WARNING above" and the two absorbing points now share one logger, (3) it keeps the record inside the `proactive_loop` namespace BY CONSTRUCTION where `type(self).__module__` would emit under `tests.test_iterNN_behavior` for a subclass defined outside the package, and (4) nothing is lost because the message already carries `self.name` and `test_iter169_behavior.py` already pins this same convention for the sibling guard by asserting `record.name == BaseCollector.__module__`, derived at runtime rather than hardcoded. VERIFIED BEFORE DECIDING: the old logger names are undocumented and untested -- neither `README.md` nor `SPEC.md` names any collector module logger (the only logger names either publishes are `proactive_loop.loop.executor` at `SPEC.md:718` and the package logger at `SPEC.md:1084`), so no published contract moves. TRAPS: the method reads `self.name` so it can never be a `staticmethod`, and it must never become an annotated class attribute (that would contribute a field to all 16 generated dataclass `__init__` signatures, which the base docstring forbids); the now-dead `_LOG` global and its `import logging` must go from both collector modules (measured: exactly one use each, at `dependencies.py:135` and `lockfile_drift.py:156`, both inside the deleted method); and the new test module name is derived from `git ls-files tests` plus one, never from the state-dir number. LAYER: L2 perception hygiene. VALUE: Med -- one concept instead of two, one docstring instead of two divergent ones, and the first oracle for a diagnostic that had none. RISK: Low -- a MOVE with no new logic, no new flag, no new config key and no dependency change, so `uv.lock` is untouched; deliberately shaped this way against a measured `stage-budget: WARN` with the engineer stage at a 600.0s median and 0.0s headroom. SOURCE: PM scout-A A1 (iter-195), lens simplification-and-deletion; its A2 (retire four void index rows) and A3 (`walk_scope`/`scan_scope`) plus scout B's B1 (lazy-import the collector registry, which arrived unpriced after that stage was killed at 600s) are all recorded as considered and declined in iter-195's `pm.md`. PRIOR STATUS AT SELECTION: n/a -- created and shipped in the same iteration. OUTCOME: SHIPPED. Row #163's `_dirs_to_scan` hoist stays QUEUED and untouched: those bodies DIFFER in behavior, so they need a parameterized delegate rather than a deletion, which is exactly why this one -- the only byte-identical duplicate with a zero-edit call-site path -- was taken first.

- **#230 -- `src/proactive_loop/collectors/base.py` states ONE precondition THREE times with TWO different numbers: lines 36 and 112 say a stray annotated class attribute would contribute a field to "all 16 generated `__init__` signatures", line 232 says "all 17", and the live registry says 17 -- so the library of a repo whose pitch is drift-guarding publicly miscounts its own collector roster, and nothing in the suite can see it.** SHIPPED in iter-196, factory iter 198, created and retired in the same commit; fields are labelled rather than piped so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row. MEASURED AT THE PM GATE, not inherited from the slate: `len(all_collectors())` is 17, the `add_parser` roster is 16 verbs of which 15 register `--json` (`watch` the sole and correct exception), and a whitespace-collapsed census of the signature-count shape over all 36 modules under `src/proactive_loop/` finds exactly THREE sites -- `base.py` 16, `base.py` 16, `base.py` 17. THE REFLEX READING IS BACKWARDS HERE: the NEWEST line is the correct one, because line 232 arrived with factory iter 197 (the `_log_absorbed` hoist) while the two older copies were left behind -- iter 195's reviewer measured this, graded it a NIT, and it shipped uncorrected, which is exactly why the fix had to arrive with an oracle instead of as another correction that decays. ENHANCEMENT: correct the two `16`s, date the one `cli.py` rationale whose "`--json` is the machine-readable idiom on 14 of the 16 verbs" was true only BEFORE `scan --json` landed in factory iter 190 (its own next clause already reads "`scan` ... WAS one of the two that refused it", so the sentence half-describes a world that no longer exists), and add `tests/test_iter201_behavior.py` binding the signature-count claim to `len(all_collectors())`. WHY THE CLI NOTE IS DATED RATHER THAN RE-COUNTED: the sibling guard `test_source_comment_bounds.py` deliberately PERMITS a dated historical measurement ("profiled at factory iter 130 on a ~190-file tree") because that is provenance, not a claim about today, so making the sentence explicitly historical keeps its WHY intact and keeps the number at 14, and that corrected sentence then serves as the new census's REAL-NEGATIVE fixture proving the guard does not eat honest history. TRAPS, each measured in this stage rather than assumed: (1) THE DERIVATION MUST BE `all_collectors()` AND NEVER `BaseCollector.__subclasses__()` -- the slate offers them as equivalent and they are not, because `tests/test_iter169_behavior.py` defines THREE `BaseCollector` subclasses (`_HealthyCollector`, `_ExplodingCollector`, `_SilentlyExplodingCollector`), so the subclass count is 17 in a bare interpreter and 20 once the suite has imported that module, making a census bound to it report correct prose as WRONG, intermittently, depending on import order and on which `pytest-xdist` worker collects it; (2) THE MATCHER MUST BE WHITESPACE-COLLAPSED ACROSS LINES -- both wrong sites wrap mid-phrase, and a line-based scan returns zero findings, which is indistinguishable from health, the fail-open shape this repo keeps rediscovering; (3) the two claim spellings DIFFER (line 36 is a comment reading "generated dataclass `__init__` signatures" in single backticks, lines 112 and 232 are docstrings reading "generated ``__init__`` signatures" in double), so a matcher pinned to one spelling silently covers one site; (4) the new module name is DERIVED from `git ls-files tests` (highest `test_iter200` -> 201, with `git cat-file -e HEAD:tests/test_iter201_behavior.py` verified to FAIL so the path is free) and never from the state-dir number 196, which would overwrite a shipped oracle. LAYER: L2 perception hygiene, documentation only. ZERO runtime lines: three prose tokens plus one new test module, no new flag, no new config key, no dependency change, so `uv.lock` is untouched and CI's `uv sync --locked` cannot drift. VALUE: Med -- the README's identical count is right because `test_readme_and_ci_contract.py` reds the build when it drifts, and this closes the same gap one layer down, inside the library a reader actually opens. RISK: Low, and deliberately shaped this way against a measured `stage-budget: WARN` with the engineer stage at a 600.0s median and 0.0s headroom, where the only safe bite is one that adds no runtime code and needs no call-site edits. - **#233 -- `dispatch` is the verb the autonomy contract makes MANDATORY for every sensitive goal -- the one a human is about to approve BY HAND -- and it is the only gated-execution verb with NO preview: measured iter-198, its flags are exactly `--slate --goal-id --yes --json`, so the only way to learn what it would do with an approved goal is to let it happen (`create_client` at `cli.py:4474`, then `_execute_goal` builds a `ToolRegistry`, writes a run dir under `state_dir` and spends real loop iterations). `run --dry-run` has previewed the AUTONOMOUS verb since iter-62, so the product previews the case where nobody is watching and does not preview the case where somebody is.** SHIPPED in iter-198, factory iter 200, created and retired in the same commit (so it never occupied the live index -- the PM must land its own record in the shipping commit, and a settled row parked in the index trips the retire-on-ship brake). Slice: one `--dry-run` flag on `p_dispatch`, declared MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE with `--json`, plus a preview block in `_cmd_dispatch` placed AFTER the gate and `--yes` checks and BEFORE `create_client`, printing the goal id and title, the decision, the resolved workspace root, the state dir the run would use, and a paste-ready real command. Two-sided proof that the return precedes client construction: the same argv with `--provider no-such-provider` exits 1 (`error: unknown provider`) without the flag and 0 with it. DECIDED, not discovered: `--dry-run --json` is a USAGE ERROR (exit 2, stdout EMPTY) because `dispatch --json` publishes ONE top-level `_dispatched_json_payload` whose nine keys are documented as ALWAYS present (`cli.py:2412-2436`, pinned by `test_iter158/163/173/174`); `run --dry-run --json` is not a counter-precedent because `_run_json_payload` already owns a nullable `dispatched` slot and `dispatch --json` has none.   FIVE PM MAINTENANCE FINDINGS FROM iter-228, recorded HERE and NOT in the index because `ROADMAP.md` sits at 39,071 of its 40,000-char ceiling and the index can no longer absorb prose (same reason as the iter-198 note above). Every figure was measured this iteration against `747c860`, never inherited. **(a) Row #194's prescribed IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE is false in both halves, which is most of why it has felt too big.** It asks for "a newline-delimited file (the `--baseline`/`--exclude-path` precedent)": `--baseline` is a JSON document read by `_load_signal_baseline` (`cli.py:2950`), `--exclude-path` is a repeatable `action="append"` flag (`cli.py:1186`), and `.splitlines()` has ZERO hits in `cli.py` -- so that file would be the FIRST line-based reader in the CLI, i.e. a new format plus a new validator plus a new fail-closed exit-2 path. A repeatable flag has a proven precedent in this exact parser and reduces the slice to ONE condition at the auto-dispatch pick, `cli.py:4680-4685`. Identity MUST key on a normalised TITLE, never `CandidateGoal.id` -- `_new_id()` is fresh every scan, so an id-keyed suppression is dead on arrival. Open trade the row still has to settle: a flag ships the GATE semantics and defers DURABILITY, a file ships durability plus the blindfold risk the row itself raises. **(b) Row #190's seam question is SETTLED by measurement, and its premise needs one correction.** Do NOT hang `--max-iterations`/`--max-llm-calls` on `globals_` (`cli.py:566`): it parents ~19 subparsers including the four zero-input catalog verbs (`policy`, `tools`, `collectors`, `providers`) whose SPEC contracts pin every inherited global INERT, so that placement contradicts four shipped contracts at once. The right shape is a SECOND `add_help=False` parent attached only to `run`/`dispatch`/`resume`, safe by construction because `_settings` (`cli.py:1657`) reads each flag through `getattr(args, name, None)` and `Settings.from_env` drops `None` overrides. CORRECTION to the row's premise, and it must be re-derived BEFORE the row is taken: `max_iterations` and `max_llm_calls` return ZERO hits in `config.py`, so the claim that `pla config --json` publishes both budgets is unproven under those names -- find the real `Settings` field names first. `_settings` today forwards only `provider`, `scripted_responses_path`, `state_dir` and `workspace_root`, so the row also owns a `_settings` edit it does not mention. **(c) Row #218 is UNDER-PRICED and three of its figures have drifted.** Live: the README configuration table is at :604 with the variable rows at :621-649 (row cites 524-556); the census site in `test_iter177` is :675 (row cites :671); and the row names ONE anti-conftest pin to retire when there are TWO -- `test_iter159:595 test_b7_no_conftest_was_added` AND `test_iter185:333 test_b09_no_conftest_exists_at_the_repo_root_or_under_tests`, the second added by a LATER iteration than the row. The full `TESTS_DIR.glob("test_*.py")` census a new `tests/conftest.py` escapes is 8 sites across 6 modules (`108:357`, `149:338`, `149:422`, `150:515`, `159:484`, `159:498`, `177:675`, `185:234`). Price the two pin retirements as a DECISION (two shipped tests assert that file must not exist), not as cleanup. **(d) Row #205's premise is largely FALSIFIED and it must not be priced High until its module list is re-derived.** It claims 7 more modules sample `git status --porcelain` on the shipped tree and names `test_iter11/52/85/137/154`; an anchored search finds exactly TWO real-tree samplers in the whole suite -- `test_iter52:382-383` (`-C str(REPO)`) and `test_iter143:138-139` (already fixed in iter-166). `test_iter85`/`test_iter137` drive a FAKE git through a monkeypatched runner, `test_iter154` makes ZERO `subprocess.run` calls, and `test_iter11_helpers` tests pure classifier functions.
