- **#234 -- `broken_link` overlap-tests the inline-code mask against a link's DESTINATION, not its whole match, so a code-formatted LABEL no longer hides a dead target** (created and shipped in iter-229, factory iter 203; deliberately never parked in the index, so there is no settled row to retire and `ROADMAP.md` absorbed only its 125-char ledger line). SOURCE: PM scout-A A1 (iter-229). LAYER: L2/integrity. VALUE: Med-High. RISK: Very Low. STATUS: SHIPPED.

  THE DEFECT. `_broken_links_in` skipped a link when the code-span ranges overlapped `match.start()`/`match.end()` -- the WHOLE `[text](target)` span -- so backticks in the link's TEXT masked the link's DESTINATION. The module's own SCOPE docstring ("a link inside a fenced block, or inside a backtick inline-code span, is a code sample and is never reported") is what made this read as intentional, and it was corrected in the same commit to say DESTINATION. `broken_link` is one of only four kinds this repo ARMS as enforcement, in all THREE gates (`Makefile:205`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:105`, `hooks/pre-commit:44`), so the fail-open sat on the most-armed kind.

  MEASURED BY THE PM IN-STAGE, not inherited from the scout. Five-arm fixture through `BrokenDocLinkCollector().collect(root)`: a plain broken link reports 1 (unchanged); the same line with a backticked label reported 0 and must report 1; a link WHOLLY inside one code span reports 0 (unchanged, and `tests/test_iter144_behavior.py:331` already pins that exact fixture, so it is the guard that the fix is not sloppy); a fenced-block link reports 0 (unchanged); a backticked-label link whose target EXISTS reports 0 (unchanged). BLAST RADIUS re-derived against HEAD BLOBS (`git show HEAD:<f>`, never the dirty tree) over all 7 tracked `.md` files: 0 findings under the old rule and 0 under the new one, with 9 backticked-label links present -- so un-blinding the collector could not red the armed public gate. The fix is one hoisted group decision plus `match.span(group)` in place of the whole-match span.

  THE LIVE INCIDENT THAT MOTIVATED IT, worth recording because it is the cost measured rather than argued. When iter-229 opened, three tracked files carried the SAME dead target `proactive-loop-agent/SPEC.md` (a foreign working-tree edit; HEAD was correct in all three) and the armed scan reported ONE of the three: `README.md:36` uses the bare spelling and was caught, while `ROADMAP.md:4` and `ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md:8` use the backticked-label idiom and were invisible. The PM restored the two files the role owns as part of this roadmap edit; the engineer restored `README.md` from HEAD. Under the shipped rule all three would have been reported.

  EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE, so a later reader does not think these were missed: no change to `_code_span_ranges` itself or to the `notes`/`todos` masks; no reference-style or raw-HTML link support; no test that scans THIS repo's own tracked `.md` files (the armed gate in `make check` and CI already does exactly that, and such a test would red the suite on any future doc edit for no coverage the fixture arms do not give); no fifth armed kind and no edit to the three byte-pinned gate command lines. Triage and full reasoning in iter-229's `pm.md`.

  THREE PM MAINTENANCE FINDINGS FROM iter-229, recorded HERE and NOT in the index because `ROADMAP.md` sits at 37,704 of its 40,000-char ceiling and the established precedent (the iter-198 and iter-228 notes above) is that new prose goes to the archive. **(a) QUEUE THIS NEXT: the graded gate asserts that a run HAPPENED and never that the right goals were REFUSED.** Measured iter-229 by scout B: `run --json` publishes `needs_approval`, `top_goal`, `slate_path` and `goal_count`, and the ONE script that consumes that document (`examples/check_run.py`) references `dispatched` 12 times and all four of those keys ZERO times -- so the product's headline safety claim is ungraded while the artifact that would prove it is already published. Non-vacuous in the bundled fixture, measured three ways with `dispatch --dry-run` (no provider, no run dir): the TOP-scored goal at 25.0 exits 4 on the sensitive-category rule, a 22.5 goal exits 0 AUTO_DISPATCH, a 1.5 goal exits 4 on the threshold. Exit codes 3 and 4 are published contract and no gate exercises them. FOUR traps to settle before coding: `needs_approval` entries carry only `id` and `title` (no category, no reason), so the property is NOT decidable from `run.json` alone and needs the same-run `slate_path` join; goal ids are per-scan random (`_new_id()`), so nothing may hardcode `--goal-id` -- match by normalised title or read ids from the same run's document; the assertion must derive the sensitive set from published policy, never a hardcoded category list, or it silently stops matching when `PLA_SENSITIVE_CATEGORIES` changes; and an expected-failure gate step must assert the SPECIFIC exit code, since exit 2 on a bad path reads exactly like the refusal being tested. Shape 1 (extend `check_run.py`, precedent iter-196) needs no gate-file change; shape 2 (a `--dry-run` gate step) buys the exit-code coverage at the price of editing both byte-pinned gate files. **(b) The four git collectors render an EMPTY workspace label under `--workspace .`, the exact spelling `make check` and CI use.** Measured iter-229 by scout A: `pla signals --workspace .` emits "Uncommitted change in : README.md" while `--workspace "$(pwd)"` emits the repo name, because the summary is built from `directory.name` and `Path(".").name` is the empty string. Census: 6 sites across 4 modules -- `git_activity.py:69`, `git_stash.py:153`, `git_state.py:121`, `working_tree.py:296`, `working_tree.py:300`, `working_tree.py:337`. This is more than cosmetic: `summary` is one of the six `_SIGNAL_IDENTITY_KEYS`, so a `--baseline` captured with `--workspace .` can never match one captured with an absolute path, silently and in the reporting-safe direction. Only `test_iter137_behavior.py` asserts either summary string and it does not cover the empty-label case, so no oracle fails today. Fix at the boundary (the `cli.py` `_relative_signal_path` precedent, rows #157/#165) and note it is itself a one-time identity ratchet break; it is the LABEL, distinct from row #158's perception SCOPE -- do not bundle them. **(c) Row #205 is now measured THREE times and scout A's iter-229 count does NOT overturn the two prior falsifications -- it counts a different thing.** Scout A reported 10 modules that "sample `git status --porcelain`" against the row's 7 and named four the row misses; that census counts TEXTUAL MENTIONS. The iter-198 and iter-228 notes above measured EXECUTION and both found only two real-tree samplers (`test_iter52:382-383` and `test_iter143:138-139`, the latter already fixed in iter-166), with `test_iter85`/`test_iter137` driving a monkeypatched fake git, `test_iter154` making zero `subprocess.run` calls and `test_iter11_helpers` testing pure classifier functions. Scout A's own caveat agrees it classified a pattern and reproduced no flake. So the standing recommendation is unchanged: RE-SCOPE OR RETIRE row #205, and whoever does it should use the discriminator already recorded here -- a per-module count of `str(REPO)`, since a module that never spells the repo root cannot be sampling it. The row's real residual stays thin: `test_iter143:139` invokes porcelain with no `-C`, so it samples whatever CWD the xdist worker inherited.

- **#235 -- `make demo` publishes the autonomy audit and `examples/check_autonomy.py` grades it from the demo's own `explain` output** (shipped iter 230, factory iter 204, `49e0091`). Recorded HERE in iter-231 because iteration 230's cap-killed engineer ordered the ledger row last and it never landed; the reviewer named the omission the loudest NIT rather than a BLOCKING finding, correctly declining to destroy 214 lines of green work over a docs row no oracle enforces. This entry closes that gap in the next PM's own commit instead of leaving it to drift -- the mechanism that permanently lost iterations 64 and 122.

- **#236 -- The per-scan work-budget oracle (#225) shipped THREE budgets and named a fourth as unenforced; two I/O seams still have no owner** (shipped iter 231, factory iter 205). `tests/test_iter192_behavior.py` pins `WALK_BUDGET`, `PARSES_PER_SOURCE_FILE` and `CHILD_PROCESS_BUDGET`, and its own header records that the `"583 -> 200 read_text"` reduction was *"true when written and unenforced since"*. Nothing anywhere bounds the `Path.stat` fan-out, which iter-231's scout measured at 1,589 calls over 318 distinct paths -- 1,271 redundant (80%) -- on a scan of this repo, a number no gate could have surfaced. Row #210 explicitly defers the `Path.stat` seam as CONVERSION work; this row is the OBSERVABILITY half, and it is deliberately independent of it: a ceiling can be pinned and defended before any collector is converted, and it is what makes a future conversion's win provable rather than asserted in a comment.

  WHY A CALL COUNT AND NEVER A DURATION. The wall-clock win `text_source.py` reports is regime-dependent (-21% one-shot, -6% warm) and unassertable in a public offline suite on a two-version CI matrix; the CALL COUNT is deterministic and machine-independent, which is exactly what this suite can own. That is also why both new budgets are UPPER BOUNDS with measured headroom rather than equalities: CPython 3.12 and 3.13 may differ by a handful of internal `stat` calls, and an equality would red one matrix leg.

  EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE, so a later reader does not think these were missed: no `src/` change of any kind; no conversion of the remaining `os.walk` collectors (row #210 -- and iter-231's scout re-measured those seven redundant traversals at ~9 ms and the stat fan-out at ~2 ms of a 538 ms scan, so they are hygiene work and must not be priced as throughput); no cross-process parse cache (row #142's open SPEC question); and no assertion on scan latency.
- **#237 -- `cli.py`'s MODULE docstring names a stale spelled verb count and enumerates 15 of the 17 live verbs, and a test PINS the stale word so the honest fix reds the build** (created and shipped in iter-232, factory iter 206; deliberately never parked in the index, so there is no settled row to retire and `ROADMAP.md` absorbed only its 118-char ledger line -- the iter-229/#234 precedent, taken because the index sits at 38,052 of its 40,000-char ceiling). SOURCE: PM scout-B B1 (iter-232). LAYER: Docs/integrity. VALUE: Med-High. RISK: Very Low. STATUS: SHIPPED.

  THE DEFECT, MEASURED BY THE PM IN-STAGE rather than inherited from the scout. `src/proactive_loop/cli.py`'s module docstring -- the first prose any reader of the CLI surface meets -- said the file wires the library "into **fifteen** verbs a person actually runs" and then enumerated scan, dispatch, run, resume, runs, explain, trace, signals, watch, diff, policy, tools, collectors, providers, config: **15 names against a live roster of 17**. Measured by word-boundary search of the live subparser roster against the docstring parsed out of the SOURCE (`ast.get_docstring`), the two absent verbs are exactly **`verify`** and **`trend`**.

  WHY THIS OUTRANKED A MEASURED SPEED WIN, and it is the transferable part: **the repo actively enforced the wrong answer.** `tests/test_iter75_behavior.py::test_b12_cli_docstring_says_fifteen_verbs` asserted `"fifteen" in doc`, so correcting the prose REDDED the build -- which is why iters 189/193/198/201, all four of them roster-integrity work, walked straight past a defect in `src/`. A wrong-way oracle is strictly worse than an unguarded stale line: it converts the honest edit into a regression and so survives every future docs pass. Sharper still, **the file already contradicted itself**: `build_parser.__doc__` opens "Assemble the ``pla`` parser with **seventeen** subcommands" and is DERIVED-guarded by `tests/test_iter79_behavior.py::test_b01_build_parser_docstring_names_live_subcommand_count`, which builds its expected phrase from the live count through a 1..20 `_NUM_WORD` map. So the mechanism this fix needs was already shipped in this very repo, applied to the FUNCTION docstring and never to the MODULE docstring above it. `tests/test_iter202_behavior.py:484` also asserts the live count is 17 AND that `trend` is a live verb, so the suite knew both halves of the contradiction.

  THE SHAPE DECISION, settled by the PM so the engineer inherits no design question: **count-plus-pointer, not a completed 17-name enumeration.** `tests/test_spec_layout_contract.py` already codifies the governing rule in its own docstring -- a roster is EMPTY-or-COMPLETE, "naming none is honest (the count plus a pointer), naming all is honest, and naming some is the shipped defect" -- and `SPEC.md:68` already carries the fixed shape (`argparse CLI: 17 verbs -- roster in 4.5`) from iter-193. A second 17-name enumeration inside a module docstring would be a second maintenance site that goes stale on the 18th verb, which is the objection iter-193 recorded when it made the same call for SPEC.md. The spelled word (`seventeen`) was kept rather than switched to a numeral because spelled counts are the established in-file convention for `cli.py` docstrings (`build_parser` "seventeen subcommands", `_cmd_tools` "the fourteen registered tools", `_cmd_collectors` "seventeen registered collectors") and a working derived guard for that convention already exists to mirror.

  THE ORACLE, PROVEN TWO-SIDED IN-STAGE BEFORE IT WAS SPECCED. The new guard reads the docstring from the SOURCE TEXT (`-OO` deletes `__doc__`, the fragility that made iter-201 repoint the README's exit-code contract at the `--help` epilog) and searches a whitespace-COLLAPSED copy, which also removes the 3.12-vs-3.13 docstring-indent difference from the question entirely. Its load-bearing rule is decidable: the longest maximal comma-separated run of whole-word live verb names in the docstring must be **0 or exactly the live count**. Measured on this tree: the shipped docstring scored a run of **4** (`scan, dispatch, run, resume`), a planted 3-name partial scored 3 and FAILS, a planted complete 17-name list scores 17 and PASSES, and the corrected prose scores 0 and PASSES. That is the property that stops the defect recurring, as opposed to merely correcting one word -- and note prose like "where run artifacts land on disk" cannot form a run, so the rule does not fight ordinary English in which eight of the verb names are also common words.

  EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE, so a later reader does not think these were missed: no change to `build_parser`'s, `_cmd_tools`' or `_cmd_collectors`' docstrings (all three already correct and already derived-guarded); no new verb, no version bump, no dependency change and so no `uv.lock` churn; no behavior or output change on any verb; and NOT bundled with row #232 (the eight stale spelled counts in test NAMES) even though `test_b12_collector_count_fifteen` sits about fifteen lines below the test being rewritten in the same module -- that row needs a two-sided sweep which must spare the CORRECT `_thirteen_promised_names_sorted` (13 `__all__`) and every `_fourteen` tool name.

  SIX PM MAINTENANCE FINDINGS HARVESTED FROM THE iter-232 SCOUT SLATES, recorded here so a later iteration does not re-buy measured-and-rejected work. **(a) The `syntax_error` hot spot is IRREDUCIBLE per-parse and the obvious route to it is FALSIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Scout A measured `compile(..., "exec")` at 269.6 ms against `PyCF_ONLY_AST` at 278.0 ms over the same 269 files -- AST-only is **3.1% SLOWER**, so the parser is the cost and codegen is noise -- and it also stops detecting **9 of 13** probed error classes (`return`/`yield` outside function, duplicate argument, module-level `nonlocal`, `await` outside async, `break`/`continue` outside loop, `import *` in a function, starred assignment). `compile()` is 56.5% of a 544 ms scan (282.3 ms over 269 calls), so this is the single biggest-looking target in the product and it is closed; the only remaining lever is a cross-process cache, which is row #142 and BLOCKED on an operator SPEC decision. **(b) A shared `line_source` cache is not worth a third scope lifetime**: the fan-out is real (278 of 280 consumed paths are read by 3 collectors each, 836 consumptions) but ONE `splitlines` pass over the whole consumed corpus costs 5.14 ms, so removing two redundant passes is ~10 ms of ~500 ms (~2%) and `syntax_error`, the largest consumer, never splits lines. **(c) Row #210's remaining traversal conversions must NOT be priced as throughput any more, and `collectors/dir_source.py`'s docstring is now actively misleading about it.** That docstring still prices the seam at "13 traversals, 3,317 directory visits, ~915 ms per scan" and "roughly a third of the scan in redundant traversal" -- true when written against a ~3,000-file workspace, but measured today the 8 `os.walk` calls cost **15.7 ms total, 3.1%** of the scan, so converting the six remaining walkers is worth ~11 ms (~2%). The row may still be worth doing for single-prune-policy hygiene; a future PM must not read those docstring numbers as a live justification, and correcting them is itself a small docs-integrity bite. **(d) NEW SITE for row #231, which no roadmap row records**: that row cites only `SPEC.md:41` for the 4-of-9 Makefile roster, and scout B measured a second independent instance at **`SPEC.md:1151-1154`** giving the same partial four (the string `1151` appears 0 times in `ROADMAP.md`). Both omit `typecheck`, `check` and `check-matrix` -- the mypy oracle, the local reproduction of the CI gate and the both-interpreter-legs run -- so a reader of the design contract concludes this project has no type oracle and no local gate. Taking row #231's fold-in half without this leaves the identical false roster three-quarters of the way down the same file. **(e) Row #232 under-reports its own finding by 8x**: it names one stale spelled collector count in a test name (`test_iter57_helpers.py:32 test_catalog_has_sixteen_entries`), and a census of spelled counts in `def test_` names found **8** stale sites whose bodies and constants all assert the live 17 -- `test_iter31:104`, `test_iter53:487`, `test_iter57:353`, `test_iter57_helpers:32`, `test_iter67:156`, `test_iter75:485`, `test_iter79:224`, `test_iter111:391` -- plus a false sentence in `test_iter31`'s docstring ("the 15 SPEC 4.1 names" while `_DOCUMENTED_COLLECTOR_NAMES` holds 17). **(f) A live one-sided hole in the Makefile/README contract**: `tests/test_makefile_readme_contract.py` reds when the README documents a target that has gone missing, but nothing reds when a real recipe is documented nowhere -- and the README documents 7 of the 9 recipes while `make setup` and `make clean` appear 0 times, with `README.md:143` handing the reader `uv sync --locked` directly instead of the `make setup` front door that exists for it.

  ONE MEASUREMENT-METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING, from scout A, because it silently changes what is being measured: **any `os.walk` shim in this repo must be LAZY or it measures a different product.** A shim doing `list(os.walk(...))` materializes the generator and so DEFEATS the caller's in-place `dirnames[:]` prune; that run reported 4,425.9 ms over 3,308 `.py` files because it had descended into `.venv` and `.git`, against 499.6 ms and 269 parses once the shim yielded one triple at a time. The corrected figure reconciles with the product's own `pla signals --timings` instrument (544.10 ms, 17 collectors, 75 signals), which is the cross-check that caught it.
- **#238 -- `pla scan --exclude-path GLOB`: the perception INPUT filter exists on the read-only preview verb and NOT on the verb that feeds the synthesizer, so 3 of the 8 top `todo` prompt slots on this repo are lines from a file the roadmap itself calls settled history** (created and shipped in iter-233, factory iter 207; deliberately never parked in the index, so there is no settled row to retire and `ROADMAP.md` absorbed only its 111-char ledger line -- the iter-229/#234 and iter-232/#237 precedent, taken because the index sits at 38,052 of its 40,000-char ceiling). SOURCE: PM scout-B B1 (iter-233). LAYER: L2/UX. VALUE: Med-High. RISK: Low. STATUS: SHIPPED.

  THE DEFECT, MEASURED BY THE PM IN-STAGE rather than inherited from the scout (every figure reproduced independently through `_collect(Path("."))` before the pick was made). `signals`, the read-only LLM-free preview verb, carries FOUR input filters (`--kind`, `--min-weight`, `--exclude-path`, `--baseline`, plus `--collector`); `scan`, the verb that actually feeds the synthesizer, carried `--collector` alone. So a user could tune what the preview perceives and could carry NONE of it into the decision that matters. The cost is **crowding-out, not token spend**, which is what makes it measurable rather than speculative: `scout/synthesizer.py:55` sets `_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_KIND = 8` and its own comment states the `[:_MAX_SIGNALS_PER_KIND]` slice "decides WHICH signals reach the model". Measured on this repo: **75** total signals, a **30**-signal `todo` pool of which only 8 reach the model, **12 of those 30 sourced from `ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md`**, and **3 of the 8 top-ranked `todo` slots held by `ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md` lines** (a 4th by `ROADMAP.md:42`, prose about signal weights rather than a real todo). `ROADMAP.md`'s own header designates that archive settled history and says never to read it wholesale, yet 37.5% of the todo evidence the scout brain reasons over was exactly that file. `--exclude-path ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md` takes 75 signals to **62**.

  WHY THIS OUTRANKED THE OTHER SLATE, stated plainly because it OVERRODE this roadmap's standing tie-break. `ROADMAP.md` says "an integrity fix (a live docs-vs-code contradiction on a public repo) outranks a net-new capability", and scout A's A1 was exactly that: nine test function names and one docstring spelling `fifteen`/`sixteen` against a live registry of 17, five of them contradicting the integer their own body asserts. It lost on two grounds. First, the **diversity guard**: A1 is a near-clone of the feature that shipped the previous iteration, `1781c88` "derive the CLI verb-count guard instead of pinning a stale roster (foundry iter 232)" -- the same class (a stale spelled count versus a live registry) with the same remedy (a derived census guard) -- and the PM role card forbids a near-clone of the previous two ships when a viable candidate from the other lens exists. Second, the tie-break is a TIE-break and leverage was not tied: A1's nine defects are all in test NAMES whose assertion bodies are already correct, so no user-visible behavior is wrong and scout A itself conceded the guard "polices style", whereas this row changes which evidence the product's core function reasons over. Row #232 (A1's slice) stays QUEUED and is explicitly NOT bundled here.

  TWO BLOCKERS SCOUT B FLAGGED, BOTH RESOLVED IN `pm.md` SO THE ENGINEER INHERITED NO DESIGN QUESTION. **(a) The VIEW-vs-INPUT doctrine did not block this.** Row #194 is about GOAL suppression reaching the gate, a different layer, and row #194's own text cites `--exclude-path` as the existing LOCATION-shaped SIGNAL-layer suppression. More decisively, `cli.py:666-668` already sanctions the class in prose: `--collector` is "An UPSTREAM allowlist on WHICH collectors feed synthesis -- the perception INPUT knob, complementing --top/--format (which shape the OUTPUT view only)". `scan` therefore already shipped a perception INPUT filter, described as one, immediately below the doctrine supposed to forbid one; narrowing by PATH is not different in kind from narrowing by COLLECTOR. **(b) No SPEC amendment was needed** -- measured, `SPEC.md` contains `exclude-path` **0** times against a same-file control of `--kind` 6 and `min-weight` 3, so no clause of the fixed vision bound this flag.

  THE ONE SEMANTIC DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO VERBS, specified because getting it backwards would have been silent. On `signals`, `cli.py:1174-1177` records that the exclusion is applied "DOWNSTREAM inside the shared `_select_signals` predicate -- never upstream in `_collect` -- so it changes what is REPORTED and never what RUNS". On `scan` the whole point is the other end: it must change what feeds SYNTHESIS. Both verbs still run every collector, because a signal's path exists only after collection, so "never what RUNS" holds on both; the difference is only that on `scan` the surviving set is what the model sees and what `--snapshot` records. The filter is therefore applied in `_cmd_scan` immediately after `snapshot = _collect(...)` and BEFORE both `_write_snapshot_document` and `synthesize`, so the prompt and the audit document inherit it BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by two call sites staying in sync -- the identical position and reasoning `--collector` already uses at `cli.py:4460-4461`, whose comment says `--snapshot` "honors --collector by construction, because `snapshot` already holds only what this scan perceived".

  ONE MATCHER, NEVER TWO. The flag reuses the shipped `_nonempty_glob` argparse validator (so an empty or whitespace-only pattern is a PARSE-time exit 2 with wording identical to `signals`, before any collector runs) and the shipped `_path_excluded` predicate (`cli.py:2858`), which carries the ancestor-directory walk, the `:LINE`-suffix tolerance, case-insensitivity, and the load-bearing rule that **a path-less signal is NEVER excluded, not even by `'*'`** -- repo-level perception must not vanish because a user excluded a directory. Neither helper was modified. A dedicated behavior asserts that `scan --exclude-path P` and `signals --exclude-path P` select the SAME signal set for the same workspace and a non-vacuous pattern, which is what forecloses the divergence this repo already has rows about (#163, #165) where two call sites grew two flavors of one policy.

  EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE, so a later reader does not think these were missed. **No `excluded: N` stdout trailer or count** -- deferred deliberately, because `json`, `csv` and `html` hold a hard purity invariant (the entire stdout is one document) so any trailer needs per-format handling plus five format tests, which is real scope against a `doctor` reading of `stage-budget: WARN ... worst is engineer at a 600.0s median (0.0s headroom)`; the `--snapshot` document is the audit surface in the meantime, which answers row #161's "silent blindfold" objection without touching stdout. **No `--min-weight`, `--baseline` or `--kind` on `scan`** -- scout B measured `--min-weight` at 75 -> 71 on this repo, a flag with no demonstrated effect, so only the filter that measured 13 shipped. **No goal-level suppression** (row #194, still blocked on its own VIEW-vs-INPUT ruling and a flag-name decision). **No `SPEC.md` edit**, and so no fix for the two SPEC gaps both scouts measured this iteration -- section 2's 8-of-15 top-level layout tree (row #231) and the `PLA_*` contract that claims completeness while naming 2 of the 8 live env vars -- because `SPEC.md` sits at 94,758 chars against row #179's ~100,000 trigger and is operator-owned. **No README change**: the verb count stays 17 and the collector count stays 17, so none of the three carve-out numbers in the human-owned intro block moved. **No new dependency**, so `uv.lock` is untouched and CI's `--locked` step cannot drift.

  THREE SCOUT FINDINGS HARVESTED HERE so a later iteration does not re-buy them. **(a) Row #232 under-reports its own finding by 8x** -- it names one stale spelled collector count (`test_iter57_helpers.py:32`) and scout A's AST census found NINE sites: five whose names contradict their own body's integer (`test_iter53:487`, `test_iter57:353`, `test_iter57_helpers:32`, `test_iter75:540`, `test_iter79:224`) and three more stale against the live 17 that stay green because they assert a DERIVED value (`test_iter67:156`, `test_iter111:391`, `test_iter31:104`), plus a false docstring sentence in `test_iter31` ("the 15 SPEC section 4.1 names"). The honest binding is name-versus-own-body, NOT name-versus-`len(all_collectors())`: `test_b04_the_other_fifteen_names_do_not_appear` legitimately computes 17-1=16, so a live-count guard would be wrong exactly where the name-vs-body guard is right. **(b) `SPEC.md:864` documents 3 of the `signals` verb's 9 flags** -- `--kind` 6 mentions and `--min-weight` 3, while `exclude-path`, `baseline`, `fail-on-kind`, `fail-over`, `--summary` and `--timings` each appear ZERO times. That is the same silence that made this row need no SPEC amendment, so it cuts both ways and belongs to the docs lens. **(c) Row #231 has a second, unrecorded site** and row #193 looks partly shipped already (`pla verify` now takes `--slate --snapshot --fail-on-unresolved`), so #193 wants re-scoping or retiring rather than re-specifying.

  TWO MEASUREMENT-METHOD NOTES WORTH KEEPING, both of which nearly produced a wrong candidate. **A single spelling can never establish an ABSENCE.** Scout A's first pass searched `README.md` for the literal `pla config` and reported `config`/`explain`/`providers`/`trend` as undocumented verbs; the README in fact documents all 17 as backticked table rows at :273-284, and all 8 `PLA_*` vars. It was one edit from entering the slate as a fabricated gap. Grep the BARE token before concluding anything is missing. **In this shell `grep` IS ripgrep, and it fails in both directions.** `grep -c` prints NOTHING (not `0`) on no match, so a count interpolated into a message renders as an empty string and reads as a formatting bug rather than a zero; `\|` alternation is a parse error while plain `|` works; and `grep -o` with alternation emits backtracked fragments that are indistinguishable from real data. Every census a claim rests on was re-run through Python `ast`/`str.count` instead.

Verbatim row text, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **`pla run` reports the AUTO_DISPATCH goals it skipped** -- `_cmd_run`'s `if ... and top is None` guard (`cli.py:4778`) keeps the FIRST auto-approved goal and drops the rest. `_render_table` DOES print a per-goal DECISION column, so the drop is not invisible -- the defect is that it is never STATED and never ACTIONABLE, while the strictly-less-safe NEEDS_APPROVAL class gets both a count and a paste-ready `pla dispatch` line. Closes that asymmetry: a count, the goals in `ranked()` order, and one paste-ready command each. Fires only at >=2 auto-approvals, so every current invocation stays byte-identical. WHY THE NARROWING IS THE WHOLE POINT: scout A ranked full `--max-goals N` as its one commit that "cannot surprise anyone", but roadmap row #192 already records THREE measured prerequisites and (a) is a HARD BLOCKER -- `SPEC.md:785,791` says auto-dispatch **the top** AUTO_DISPATCH goal and prints **the single** goal `--dry-run` would run, and `SPEC.md` is operator-owned, so only an operator may widen it. (c) is also live: `examples/scripted_responses.json` holds exactly 8 tag-ordered entries, so a 2nd goal's `plan` call raises `ScriptExhaustedError` and the bundled demo HARD-FAILS at N=2. This accounting slice pays none of the three -- it dispatches exactly one goal, adds no `run --json` key (the six-key document pinned at `test_iter158_behavior.py:53` is untouched), and needs no new scripted entry -- while delivering the user-facing half of #192's value. Row #192 keeps the DISPATCH half, still blocked on (a). TWO MEASURED FACTS THAT SIZED IT: `_cmd_run` runs its whole body inside `redirect_stdout(sys.stderr)` under `--json` and its docstring states that no print site added later can leak into the JSON stream, so the new block CANNOT reach `.pla_runs/run.json` -- `make demo`, `examples/check_run.py` and `examples/check_autonomy.py` are structurally unaffected. And `test_iter62_behavior.py` behavior 8 compares rendered dry-run vs real-run output with goal ids and state dirs normalised away, which is why `--dry-run` MUST print the same block: that oracle goes red if it does not. - **#117 -- Packaging-contract oracle: bind the DECLARED `[project.scripts]` string to the source object it names** (retired from the index in iter-241, foundry iter 241). Verbatim index text as it stood at retirement, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **Packaging-contract oracle** -- `[project.scripts] pla = "proactive_loop.cli:main"` and the `py.typed` marker are asserted by ZERO oracles (CI and every local run use the SOURCE tree), so renaming `main` or dropping the marker from the wheel stays green. Must stay OFFLINE: assert the declared entry point resolves to a real callable + the marker is in the built package file list; a real `pip install` needs network and is FORBIDDEN. Shrinks to entry-point resolution alone if the marker already ships. LAYER: Packaging/integrity. VALUE: Med. RISK: Low. SOURCE: PM scout-A A3 (iter-104). STATUS: **QUEUED -- the "ZERO oracles" premise is FALSIFIED in BOTH halves** (iter-163 scout A, checked at runtime, not by grep): `test_iter114_behavior.py::test_b11` locates the INSTALLED `pla` wrapper and runs `pla config --json` as a SUBPROCESS asserting exit 0 + a non-empty JSON object, and `test_iter142_behavior.py:463` asserts `(SRC_PKG / "py.typed").is_file()`. As written this row buys DUPLICATE coverage. The residual is real but different in KIND and worth less: no test reads the DECLARED `pyproject.toml` string, so the oracle resolves whatever the last `uv sync` installed and a stale `.venv` gives green. Slice, if ever taken = `tomllib` the declaration (`test_iter05_behavior.py:26` precedent), import the module part, `getattr` the attribute, assert it IS `proactive_loop.cli.main`.. SHIPPED -- foundry iter 241, as ENTRY-POINT RESOLUTION ONLY, which is the residual the row itself scopes ("shrinks to entry-point resolution alone if the marker already ships"). WHY THE OTHER HALF WAS DROPPED RATHER THAN DEFERRED: the `py.typed` half is already covered (`tests/test_iter142_behavior.py:463` asserts the marker is a file, and `tests/test_iter58_behavior.py` is a dedicated module), and the "in the BUILT package file list" phrasing needs a wheel build, i.e. NETWORK, which the product quality bar forbids -- so no future iteration should re-buy it. WHAT SHIPPED: `tests/test_iter220_behavior.py`, one offline oracle that parses the declaration with `tomllib`, splits `module:attribute`, imports the module part and `getattr`s the attribute, then asserts the result IS (identity) `proactive_loop.cli.main`. The resolver takes the declaration as an ARGUMENT so it can be falsified, and it is driven by three negative controls (absent module -> ImportError, absent attribute -> AttributeError, no-colon and whitespace shapes rejected before any import). THE GAP IT CLOSED, restated because the row's original premise was falsified twice and the residual is narrow: the two pre-existing packaging oracles both grade the INSTALLED wrapper as a subprocess (`test_iter114_behavior.py:502`, `test_iter155_behavior.py:65`), so they grade whatever the last `uv sync` left in `.venv` and a renamed `cli.main` stayed green until somebody rebuilt the venv. Honest value: hardening, no user-facing capability. Reasoning: iter-241 `pm.md`.

So a supervising script is told about the goals it may NOT act on without a human (`needs_approval`) and is blind to the goals the gate has ALREADY approved and the run merely did not reach -- the only class it may dispatch unattended. Closed with a STRICT MIRROR of a `needs_approval` entry: `[{id, title}]`, in `ranked()` order, ALWAYS present and `[]` when the gated slate holds fewer than two auto-approved goals.. LAYER: CLI/autonomy + integration. VALUE: Med-High. RISK: Low. SOURCE: PM scout-A A1 (iter-242). STATUS: **SHIPPED iter 242** (foundry iter 242). WHAT THE PM SETTLED so the engineer inherited no design question: (a) the entry shape is a strict mirror of `needs_approval` -- `{id, title}`, no paste-ready command string -- so the document gains no shape its surface does not already publish; (b) the six coupled contract sites were enumerated with line numbers instead of left to a search (`cli.py:2587` docstring, `test_iter158_behavior.py` `_TOP_KEYS` plus three -six- messages, `test_iter213_behavior.py:104-111` `_JSON_KEYS` asserted exactly at `:524`, `README.md:270` and `README.md:437-441` -- both BELOW the human-owned marker at `README.md:23` -- and the PROSE-ONLY number in `examples/check_run.py:15`, whose `status in document` discriminator at `:91` still holds and must not be touched); (c) TWO scout claims were CORRECTED by measurement -- `SPEC.md` is NOT a coupled site (it never states this document's key set, so no operator amendment is needed, unlike the `SPEC.md:785,791` hard blocker row #192 still carries), and `tests/test_iter174_behavior.py:295-305` builds a deliberately PARTIAL document with no exact-key assertion, so it is not coupled either; (d) the >=2-auto-approved slate reuses `test_iter213_behavior.py`'s proven `_scripted_file()` harness and its `MAIN_GOALS` (3 AUTO_DISPATCH + 2 NEEDS_APPROVAL) rather than inventing one, which is what sized the bite against an engineer stage measured at a 600.0s median with 0.0s headroom; (e) the PM made the three bookkeeping edits ITSELF -- this bullet, the Done-ledger row, and the `test_iter214_behavior.py:352` retirement-bullet total 72 -> 73 that this very bullet moves -- so the engineer inherits a green tree and spends its whole budget on the feature. DELIBERATELY OUT OF SCOPE: extending `examples/scripted_responses.json` to synthesize a second auto-approved goal so `_render_deferred_dispatches` finally fires in `make demo`/`make check`/CI. Scout A measured the bundled slate at needs_approval 2 / auto_dispatch 1 / blocked 1, so `deferred` is `[]` on the demo and expected behavior 2 is exactly the oracle for that; changing that fixture is a coupled edit every graded step and `examples/check_autonomy.py` read, and it is its own row. Reasoning: iter-242 `pm.md`

- **#247 -- Retire the stale `path:LINE` evidence anchors in the live index, behind a two-sided ban** (BORN RETIRED in iter 244, foundry iter 244: this row never entered the live index, because the conversion and its guard shipped in the same commit, and `settled_rows_needing_retirement` reds the build on a SHIPPED index row that is not archived in the same commit). Verbatim detail, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **`ROADMAP.md` cites evidence by path and symbol, never by line number** -- `src/proactive_loop/cli.py` is over 6,000 lines and nearly every iteration edits it, so a `path:LINE` anchor is stale on arrival and a TRUE claim reads as FABRICATED. Measured at HEAD a37d3eb: 39 backticked `TOKEN:DIGITS` locators (35 distinct) plus 4 other-spelling stragglers (the bare-name `README:524-556`, the compound `cli.py:1232 _relative_signal_path`, and the orphan continuation tokens `:422` and `:498`) = 43 sites, of which 9 were demonstrably stale: row #170 cited `cli.py:780` for a flag whose first real occurrence was line 351, and row #192 cited `cli.py:4043-4045` plus the code shape `and top is None`, which occurred ZERO times in the file, with its own earlier self-correction to `cli.py:4778` stale as well. Every converted row already carried a backticked SYMBOL in the same cell, so the fix was DELETION of a `:NNN` suffix, never the invention of an anchor. Layer: DX/integrity. Value: Med. Risk: Very Low. Source: PM scout-B B1 (iter 244). Status: SHIPPED -- iter 244 (foundry iter 244). Guard: tests/test_iter223_behavior.py -- pure text -> verdict, two-sided in BOTH directions (fires on the 4 real pre-fix spellings as synthetic strings; accepts 6 real colon-bearing tokens byte-identical, so a threshold list such as `PLA_CATEGORY_MIN_SCORE=career:4.5,project:3.0` and a pytest node id are not read as line numbers) with NO exemption on the ban, plus a cited-path census that resolves every extension-bearing token against `git ls-files` behind a self-cleaning declared-absent map. The symbol-RESOLUTION half of the scout's proposal was deliberately DROPPED, not deferred: "some backticked symbol in the row occurs in the cited file" proves existence, not uniqueness (row #170's `--fail-on-kind` occurs 28 times), so it would dress the ban up as stronger evidence than it is. Domain is `ROADMAP.md` ONLY -- this archive is a point-in-time record, so rewriting its anchors would falsify it, and the guard asserts its own read targets structurally.

- **#249 -- `run --exclude-path GLOB`: the LOCATION half of the `run` perception filter** (retired from the index in iter-249, foundry iter 249). Verbatim index text as it stood at retirement, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **`run --exclude-path GLOB` -- the LOCATION half of the `run` perception filter, deferred from iter-245 WITH its analysis so it is not re-bought** -- iter-245 shipped the `--collector` half on `p_run` as row #248 and left this one unbought deliberately. Measured then, do NOT re-derive: the CODE is ~5 lines and its position is already settled -- `_path_excluded` and the `model_copy(update={"signals": kept})` block both already exist in `_cmd_scan`, and the filter goes after `_collect` and ABOVE `_write_snapshot_document`, so `--snapshot` and `--dry-run` inherit it by construction rather than by two call sites staying in sync. The cost is DOCS, not code: `--exclude-path` occurs ZERO times in `SPEC.md` for ANY verb (neither `scan`'s nor `signals`' is specified there), so documenting it on `run` either ships an asymmetry (run specified, scan not) or must first settle whether SPEC enumerates flags at all or states one `--help` pointer -- a scoping decision, not a code question. LAYER: CLI/L2. VALUE: Med. RISK: Low. SOURCE: PM-lead (iter-245, deferred half of #248). STATUS: **QUEUED -- settle the SPEC flag-documentation scope FIRST; the code and its position are already priced** SHIPPED as foundry iter 249: the code landed as specified (one `p_run.add_argument` delegating to the EXISTING `_nonempty_glob` validator, and one `if args.exclude_path:` block in `_cmd_run` delegating to the EXISTING `_path_excluded` predicate, so no second matcher and no new module-level symbol), and the row's stated gate was settled by the PM rather than by the engineer: `--exclude-path` occurs ZERO times in `SPEC.md` for EITHER verb that already ships it, so documenting it on `run` ships no asymmetry and `SPEC.md` (operator-owned) was NOT touched. The one thing the row did not price: `_cmd_run` already ANNOUNCES its `--collector` narrowing because "`run` is the verb that ACTS, so what it was allowed to see is a safety fact", so mirroring `_cmd_scan` alone would have left `run` reporting HALF its narrowing -- a second, separate announce line was therefore part of the diff, which keeps `--collector`-only stdout byte-identical.

- **#161 -- Give `--baseline` its first consumer** (retired from the index in iter-265, foundry iter 264). Verbatim index text as it stood at retirement, pipes replaced by field labels so this bullet cannot be parsed as a table row: ENHANCEMENT: **Give `--baseline` its first consumer: commit a snapshot and arm `ci_config` only** -- the ratchet shipped as row #150 and nothing consults it; the Makefile's "red on arrival" reasoning for the 3 unarmed kinds is now stale. Measured iter-133: of those three only `ci_config` is safely armable (stable identity, and the no-CI case emits a DIFFERENT summary, so a baselined entry becomes a real "the workflow was deleted or renamed" detector); `test_posture` churns BY CONSTRUCTION (its count is inside `summary`, one of the 6 identity keys) so arming it is a self-inflicted public red build; `lockfile_drift` is mtime-driven, baseline-safe but worthless as enforcement. Needs a stated refresh rule or the committed snapshot rots into a blindfold. LAYER: L2/enforcement. VALUE: Med. RISK: Med. SOURCE: PM scout-B B2 (iter-133). STATUS: **QUEUED**. SHIPPED as foundry iter 264 by a DIFFERENT mechanism than the row proposed: the committed snapshot and the `ci_config`-only arming are deliberately NOT adopted. `make check` and ci.yml instead round-trip the snapshot `make demo` just wrote (`--workspace examples/fixture_workspace --baseline .pla_runs/snapshot.json --fail-over 0`), so the produce and the consume happen inside ONE gate run. That retires this row's own blocking objection rather than paying it -- a same-run document has no refresh rule to state and nothing to rot into a blindfold -- and it leaves the per-kind arming analysis above unspent for any future caller that does want a committed baseline.
